Omissions the checker caught:

- The PI-08 chain has a 23rd operator script the map never mentions: rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/corpus/private/build_working_manifest.py (6,655 bytes, 2026-09-07). It generates corpus_manifest.working.json — the input corpus_manifest.py validates — so the governance story is incomplete without it. It is gitignored and outside scripts/, which is presumably why it was missed.
- Actual on-disk state of the PI-08 artifacts, which answers three of the map's openQuestions: evaluation/corpus/private/ holds 12 CCSICT-*.pdf (006 absent by design, matching corpus_manifest.py:29-31), an excluded/ subdir, redacted/ with 12 derivatives + receipts, corpus_manifest.working.json and a .bak. corpus_manifest.working.json is status 'draft', 12 papers, approvals null, index_fingerprint_sha256 null; its source_sha256 matches the receipts 12/12 but redacted_sha256 matches 0/12 (still unfilled, per build_redacted_derivatives.py:175-176). There is no .locked.json and no receipt anywhere, so `lock` and `verify` have never been run.
- The committed template confirms the map's PROGRAM_SPECIALIZATIONS gotcha and answers its open question: rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/corpus/corpus_manifest.template.json:23-24 uses "program": "BSCS", "specialization": "Data Mining" (the legacy label), and so does the live working manifest. The catalog code is 'DM' (services/catalog.py:8, catalog_live_smoke.py:23).
- gemini_release_smoke.py constructs ChatGoogleGenerativeAI and GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings directly (lines 46-68) and never touches services.gemini_pool, so the only script in the container image exercises neither the reserve-key pool nor the LLM gateway — its SUMMARY.md line 'Route: direct Google API' (verify-pi03-gemini.ps1:72) is accurate but means the routed path is entirely unsmoked. It also hardcodes max_output_tokens=256 (chat) and 64 (verdict) instead of settings.gemini_max_output_tokens, so the generation_contract the release manifest records is not the contract the smoke tested.
- Evidence-file encoding is inconsistent across the five root verifiers: verify-pi03-gemini.ps1:82-83 writes SUMMARY.md as UTF-8 without BOM and with LF explicitly ('matching .gitattributes (eol=lf) so the hashes recorded below stay valid'), while verify-pi03-container-security.ps1:102, verify-pi03-dependencies.ps1:116, verify-pi03-multiarch.ps1:175 and verify-pi04-live.ps1:70 all use Set-Content -Encoding utf8, which emits a BOM under Windows PowerShell 5.1. Four of the five evidence bundles therefore carry BOM'd SUMMARY.md files whose hashes were computed over those BOMs.
- record_backup_run.ps1 has a second refusal the map omits: lines 74-78 reject a backup folder containing no artifact other than sha256-manifest.json.
- reindex_citations surfaces two argument errors as uncaught tracebacks rather than clean exits: main() raises ValueError for --allow-model-change without --apply (lines 317-318), and run_apply raises for --older-than-days < 1 (lines 264-265). Neither is caught, so both exit 1 with a stack trace.
- repair_chunk_sections and rescan_duplication both count len(papers) over every row they fetched, including rows that reported status 'error', so the closing totals (repair_chunk_sections.py:128, rescan_duplication.py:111) overstate the population actually examined — a sharper version of the map's 'understates that 16 rows were written' gotcha.
- corpus_manifest lock has a pre-flight existence check at lines 369-370 in addition to the open(mode='x') in _write_json_exclusive, so it refuses before computing the digest as well as at write time. The map mentions only the mode='x' path.

## 2. `evaluation` - Objective-2 evaluation harness, JMeter and evidence exports

This subsystem produces the paper's Objective 2 finding (does RAG beat an unaugmented Gemini baseline on the same forty faculty-validated questions?) and its Objective 4 performance/quality evidence. `run_comparison.py` drives every Golden Dataset query through two arms concurrently — a bare `ChatGoogleGenerativeAI` call and the real deployed guest `/chat` pipeline via `_chat_impl` — scores both with Ragas Answer Correctness, adds RAG-only Faithfulness and Context Precision, and applies the paper's Section 3.2.5 statistical treatment pooled and per corpus-coverage stratum. Around that sit a chunk-size sweep that retro-justifies the 800-token constant, two JMeter summarizers with deliberately different semantics, SonarQube/LangSmith exporters, a dated evidence ledger (`iso25010_evidence.md`), and a generator that turns the single formal run into a committed JSON the admin UI renders. Everything is built so a published number can be traced back to one exact configuration: the release manifest is recorded beside the checkpoint and a resume under a changed configuration is refused.

### Key files

- `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py` (1040 lines) - The Objective 2 harness. Dataset validation, two-arm execution, retry/outage handling, checkpointing, resume provenance guard, Ragas scoring, statistics, results JSON+CSV.
- `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/golden_dataset.json` (63 KB) - The instrument: 40 queries, each with id/category/query_type/question/ground_truth/source_thesis/corpus_coverage/coverage_basis, plus `validated_by_faculty_panel`, a 3-member `validation.panel`, `validation.workflow` and `validation.instrument_revisions`. Strata: 16 present / 17 absent_topic / 4 absent_unreleased / 3 absent_by_design. Its SHA-256 prefix `5e8fb7f21db6` is the formal run id.
- `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/dev_smoke_dataset.json` (1.6 KB) - 3 fully synthetic queries, `validated_by_faculty_panel: false`, empty panel. Only usable with `--allow-unvalidated`. Its digest `e9591c80b9f1` is the checkpoint namespace behind the F10 finding.
- `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/chunk_size_sweep.py` (314 lines) - Offline retrieval-only sweep over chunk sizes 400/600/800/1000/1200 against the PI-08 corpus; writes hit@1, hit@5, MRR, mean top similarity. Never touches Supabase.
- `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/summarize_jmeter.py` (113 lines) - Generic JTL summarizer: per-label and overall latency percentiles, throughput, error rate, response-code histogram, max concurrent threads; `--require-response-code` gate.
- `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/summarize_chat_load.py` (164 lines) - /chat-specific JTL classifier. Bands samples into answer / capacity_notice / ambiguous_fast / failure by elapsed-ms thresholds, because /chat returns HTTP 200 for provider exhaustion.
- `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/export_sonar.py` (109 lines) - Pulls SonarQube measures, quality gate, unresolved issue count, bug/vuln list and new-code issues over the web API using SONAR_TOKEN as HTTP Basic user.
- `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/export_langsmith.py` (60 lines) - Privacy-filtered LangSmith run export: latency and token counts only; raises if any run carries input/output payload.
- `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md` (1243 lines / 94 KB) - The dated evidence ledger. Newest block first. Holds the 2026-09-13 formal-run tables, the /chat load profile, the F10 resume finding, the corpus release / stratum re-scope, artifact SHA-256 tables and required commands. Pinned by tests/test_readme_accuracy.py; docs/deck/build.py scrapes Objective 4 figures out of it by regex.
- `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/requirements-eval.txt` (12 lines) - ragas==0.4.3, google-genai==2.13.0, openai==2.49.0, datasets==5.0.0, pandas==3.0.5, scipy==1.18.0, langchain-community==0.4.1. Deliberately outside the production image and outside requirements.lock.
- `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/comparison_20260913_064053.json` (309 KB) - The one formal run. 40/40 scored, formal_result true. Contains rows (sanitized), ragas raw scores, statistics, means, by_corpus_coverage, rag_diagnostics, reproducibility.release.
- `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/checkpoints/5e8fb7f21db6.{pathways,scores}.jsonl + .provenance.json` (1.8 MB / 7 KB / 2.5 KB) - Resume state for the formal run. pathways.jsonl is one JSON row per completed query (1.8 MB, holds raw retrieved context); scores.jsonl is one {id,baseline,rag} per scored query; provenance.json is the 9-key answering configuration.
- `rag-thesis-backend/scripts/release_fingerprint.py` (158 lines) - `build_manifest()` — schema_version 4, git_commit, runtime, models, prompt_version, generation_contract, generation_route, rag_contract, index_fingerprint, input_sha256 over 11 files. Also `sha256_file`, used by the harness for the dataset digest.
- `rag-thesis-backend/scripts/export_objective2_summary.py` (156 lines) - Turns the pinned formal run into rag-thesis-frontend/src/data/objective2Summary.json (5 stratum rows + diagnostics + run provenance). SOURCE, RUN_ID, RUN_DATE, EVIDENCE_PATH and QUOTED_STRATUM are hardcoded constants. UNTRACKED in git as of this reading.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_objective2_summary_export.py` (118 lines) - Pins the committed JSON byte-for-byte against build(), plus LF serialization, run provenance, pooled figures (0.2169→0.3024, p=3.222e-05), and that the quoted `present` stratum is NOT significant (n=16, +0.0601, p=0.1257). UNTRACKED in git.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_evaluation_harness.py` (670 lines) - ~40 tests over validate_formal_dataset, the shipped strata counts, _ranked_contexts, sanitize_evaluation_rows, unattempted/retry semantics, checkpoint + provenance guard, statistical_treatment contract, rank-biserial, and the notice/answer diagnostic split. scipy-dependent tests are skipped via find_spec.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_jmeter_summary.py` (44 lines) - Pins percentile interpolation, error rate, max_concurrent_threads, require_response_codes, and the multi-run throughput denominator.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_evaluation_exports.py` (62 lines) - Pins the LangSmith payload rejection and the Sonar hotspot-from-component-measure workaround.
- `rag-thesis-backend/jmeter/provider_independent_load.jmx` (23 lines) - 20 users x 5 loops x 3 GETs (/health, /upload/tracks, /analytics/summary), 30 s ramp, no provider in the path. The README calls this the figure to quote for Performance Efficiency.
- `rag-thesis-backend/jmeter/chat_load.jmx` (40 lines) - Live guest RAG chat. 5 users x 3 loops default, 15 s ramp, 60 s response timeout, per-thread `X-Guest-ID: chat-load-guest-${__threadNum}`, question overridable by -JQUESTION.
- `rag-thesis-backend/jmeter/rate_limit_test.jmx` (8 lines) - 20 users x 3 loops of POST /chat {"question":"Hello"} — the greeting fast path, no Gemini. No X-Guest-ID header, so all callers share the IP-derived limiter key.
- `rag-thesis-backend/jmeter/live_gemini_smoke.jmx` (8 lines) - 1 user, 1 loop, one real RAG question, 120 s timeout, on_sample_error=stopthread.
- `rag-thesis-backend/jmeter/thesis_load_test.jmx` (150 lines) - Superseded original. Hardcoded placeholder JWT in User Defined Variables, an embedded SummaryReport ResultCollector writing relative `results/summary.csv`. README and iso25010 both say do not use.
- `rag-thesis-backend/jmeter/README.md` (81 lines) - Invocation runbook. Says to call ApacheJMeter.jar not jmeter.bat (the .bat mangles -J properties and ends in `pause`), quote each -J arg, point /chat runs at a second port bound to the disposable project.
- `rag-thesis-frontend/src/data/objective2Summary.json` (~120 lines) - Generated, committed, consumed by AdminOverview.jsx. UNTRACKED directory (src/data/) in git as of this reading.
- `rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminOverview.jsx:195-290` (modified, uncommitted) - Renders the five-row stratum table with a gold 'the figure the paper quotes' badge on `present` and a prose 'How to read this' caveat.

### Control flow

`main()` (run_comparison.py:827) parses args, loads the dataset JSON, reads `dataset['queries']` unguarded, then calls `validate_formal_dataset`. Any issue aborts via `parser.error` unless `--allow-unvalidated`, which only downgrades to a printed warning. The run id defaults to `sha256_file(dataset_path)[:12]` (:884) and names three sibling files under `results/checkpoints/`: `<id>.pathways.jsonl`, `<id>.scores.jsonl`, `<id>.provenance.json`. `--fresh` unlinks all three (:890-892). `build_manifest()` is then called exactly once (:898) so the same dict both gates the resume and is published as `reproducibility.release`. `guard_checkpoint_provenance` (:449) projects the manifest down to the nine `_ANSWERING_CONFIGURATION_KEYS` (schema_version, runtime, models, prompt_version, generation_contract, generation_route, rag_contract, index_fingerprint, input_sha256 — `git_commit` deliberately excluded). If no checkpoint file exists it writes that projection and returns; if a checkpoint exists but the provenance file does not it errors; otherwise it diffs, expanding `input_sha256` into per-file keys, and `parser.error`s naming each moved key. This is the F10 fix: before it, a re-run replayed old answers from disk while stamping the current manifest over them.

`run_pathways` (:527) → `asyncio.run(_run_pathways(...))` loads the pathways checkpoint into an id→row dict (`_load_checkpoint`, :392, one JSON object per line) and iterates queries **serially**, appending each finished row to the checkpoint file before moving on. Per query, `_run_query` (:347) loops up to `_UNATTEMPTED_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 4`. Every attempt first calls `chat_notices.reset_capacity_limit()` — without it a cooldown tripped by an earlier query makes `_chat_impl` return the capacity notice with no provider call, so a "retry" would just replay the notice. `_attempt_pathways` (:249) then `asyncio.gather`s both arms concurrently: the baseline goes through `gemini_pool.arun(baseline_llm, gemini_pool.CHAT, client.ainvoke(BASELINE_PROMPT))` — routed through the key pool deliberately, so reserve-key rotation is symmetric across arms; the RAG arm calls `_chat_impl(ChatRequest(question, department_filter=settings.thesis_evaluation_department), None, BackgroundTasks(), None, evaluation_trace=trace)`, i.e. the real production function with `request=None` and `user=None` (guest). `_chat_impl` (routers/chat.py:2075) stamps `response.kind` via `chat_notices.response_kind` and, at chat.py:2615-2621, fills the trace with `{context, sources, top_similarity}` — but only inside the retrieval `try` block, so any fast path (greeting/identity/capabilities/courtesy), guard refusal, or guest-budget/capacity short-circuit leaves the trace empty.

Back in `_run_query`: a raised exception on attempt < 4 sleeps `_UNATTEMPTED_BACKOFF_SECONDS[min(attempt-1, 2)]` = 20/45/90 s and continues; on attempt 4 it returns `_unattempted_row` (:322) with empty answers, `rag_kind: None`, `rag_unattempted: True` and a truncated `failure` string. A successful attempt whose RAG answer is *not* an unattempted notice returns `_build_row` immediately. An answer that **is** the capacity or guest-budget notice is retried; after four such attempts the last pair is recorded with `unattempted=True`. Crucially `NO_RELEVANT_PREFIX`, the grounded fallback and guard refusals are **not** in `_UNATTEMPTED_NOTICES` (:89-92) — those are scored and never retried, which is what makes the three negative controls meaningful.

`main` then computes `uncovered` (rows whose `corpus_coverage` is not in `DECLARABLE_COVERAGE`, only reachable on a resumed pre-stratum checkpoint) and `unattempted`, prints a warning for each, and sets `formal_result = not dataset_issues and not unattempted and not uncovered` (:952). Rows are sanitized (raw chunk text swapped for count + SHA-256 list) before anything is written.

Unless `--skip-ragas`, `score_with_ragas` (:689) builds a Ragas 0.4.3 judge: `llm_factory(settings.gemini_verdict_model, provider='openai', client=AsyncOpenAI(api_key=settings.gemini_api_key, base_url='https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/openai/'), max_tokens=16384)` plus `GoogleEmbeddings(genai.Client(...), model=settings.gemini_embed_model.removeprefix('models/'))`. The OpenAI-compatible endpoint is a workaround: ragas 0.4.3's native google provider wraps `instructor.from_genai` synchronously so `ascore()` cannot run. The 16 384-token bound exists because the judge's structured decomposition output scales with answer length and the *baseline* arm produces the longest answers; at the provider default, instructor raised `IncompleteOutputException` deterministically and burned all four retries (observed 2026-09-12 on a 4 420-char baseline answer). It is documented as not a measurement parameter — 4 096 and 16 384 both returned 0.1845 across three runs each. Scoring iterates rows in order, resuming from `<id>.scores.jsonl`. An `rag_unattempted` row is skipped entirely (all metrics None). Otherwise: `AnswerCorrectness.ascore(user_input, reference, response=baseline_answer)` then the same for `rag_answer`; if `rag_contexts` is non-empty, `Faithfulness.ascore(user_input, response, retrieved_contexts)` (reference-free) and `ContextPrecision.ascore(user_input, reference, retrieved_contexts)`. Every judge call goes through the local `judged()` helper, which retries 4 times on the same 20/45/90 backoff and re-raises on the fourth.

Statistics (:966-1010) loop over the single-element tuple `('answer_correctness',)`. Pairs are zipped `strict=True` across rows / baseline scores / rag scores and dropped unless both values are non-None and `math.isfinite`. The pooled pairs give `statistics.answer_correctness = statistical_treatment(base, rag)` and `means.answer_correctness`, then the same treatment runs once per stratum in `CORPUS_COVERAGE` order into `by_corpus_coverage`. `statistical_treatment` (:761) refuses on <3 pairs or on all-identical differences, then runs `scipy.stats.shapiro(diffs)`; `p > 0.05` selects `ttest_rel(rag, baseline)`, otherwise `wilcoxon(rag, baseline)`. The 95 % interval is always a two-sided Student's t interval on the mean paired difference (`t.ppf(0.975, n-1) * sd/sqrt(n)`) — it sits outside the parametric branch, so a rank test still gets a t interval — and both effect sizes (Cohen's d_z and matched-pairs rank-biserial) are always emitted so the output shape is stable. `summarize_rag_diagnostics` (:714) then pairs each rag score with its source row and reports each of faithfulness/context_precision pooled plus split by `rag_kind`, so the eight notice rows that score near zero while still carrying five contexts do not silently depress the grounding figure. Finally the JSON is written with `allow_nan=False` and pandas writes the CSV if importable.

Downstream: `scripts/export_objective2_summary.py` reads the one pinned run file, refuses if `results/checkpoints/5e8fb7f21db6.provenance.json` is missing, flattens pooled + four strata into rows carrying baseline/rag/n/delta/p_value/significant/cohens_d_z/ci_95/quoted, and writes LF-terminated JSON into `rag-thesis-frontend/src/data/objective2Summary.json`, which `AdminOverview.jsx:205-285` renders as a seven-column table with a gold badge on the quoted `present` row. `tests/test_objective2_summary_export.py` asserts `committed == build()` plus the exact pooled and present-stratum figures, so regenerating from a different run breaks the build and forces Section 3.2.5 to be revisited.

The JMeter side is unconnected to run_comparison: JTLs are produced out-of-band and fed to one of two summarizers. `summarize_jmeter.load_runs` tags each file with `_run` so per-file durations are summed rather than spanning idle gaps between runs, groups by `label`, and emits per-endpoint and overall blocks; `require_response_codes` raises before the file is written, which is how the rate-limit profile is refused unless a real 429 was observed. `summarize_chat_load.classify` ignores the `success` column entirely and bands purely on responseCode + elapsed: non-200 → failure; 200 and <100 ms → capacity_notice; 200 and <2 000 ms → ambiguous_fast; else answer. Percentiles are reported over the answer band only, with n stated.

### Public surface

- `python -m evaluation.run_comparison [--dataset PATH] [--skip-ragas] [--allow-unvalidated] [--run-id ID] [--fresh]` — run_comparison.py:827-1040. Writes evaluation/results/comparison_<UTC %Y%m%d_%H%M%S>.{json,csv}.
- `run_comparison.validate_formal_dataset(dataset) -> list[str]` (:157-205). Returns every blocking condition: 30<=len(queries)<=50; unique ids; non-empty question; ground_truth and source_thesis present and not starting `REPLACE:`; corpus_coverage in DECLARABLE_COVERAGE; corpus_coverage != 'undetermined'; validated_by_faculty_panel is True; exactly 3 panel members; each with non-empty name/position/date_validated; 3 distinct casefolded names; ISO YYYY-MM-DD dates.
- `run_comparison.is_unattempted(answer) -> bool` (:95-97). Whitespace-normalizes then prefix-matches the first 60 chars of CAPACITY_MESSAGE or GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE only.
- `run_comparison._ranked_contexts(context) -> list[str]` (:207-220). Splits the prompt context on `^\[(\d+)\][^\n]*\n` headers, slices bodies between consecutive headers, returns bodies sorted by citation number — restoring pre-LongContextReorder rank.
- `run_comparison.sanitize_evaluation_rows(rows) -> list[dict]` (:223-238). Drops `rag_context` and `rag_contexts`, substitutes `retrieved_context_count` and `retrieved_context_sha256` (list of per-chunk SHA-256).
- `run_comparison.run_pathways(queries, checkpoint=None) -> list[dict]` (:527) / `_run_pathways` (:500-524) / `_run_query` (:347-385) / `_attempt_pathways` (:249-279).
- `run_comparison.score_with_ragas(rows, checkpoint=None) -> {'baseline': [...], 'rag': [...]}` (:689) / `_score_with_ragas` (:561-685).
- `run_comparison.statistical_treatment(baseline_scores, rag_scores) -> dict` (:761-825). Keys: shapiro_wilk{statistic,p_value,normal}, test, statistic, p_value, significant_at_0.05, n_pairs, mean_difference, mean_difference_ci_95{lower,upper,method}, effect_size{cohens_d_z{value,definition}, rank_biserial_correlation{value,definition}}. Or `{'note': ...}` in two short-circuit cases.
- `run_comparison.summarize_rag_diagnostics(rows, rag_scores) -> dict` (:714-756). Per metric: mean, n, not_applicable, answers_only{mean,n}, notices_only{mean,n}.
- `run_comparison._rank_biserial_correlation(diffs) -> float|None` (:693-711). (W+ - W-)/(W+ + W-) over non-zero differences with `scipy.stats.rankdata` average ranks.
- `run_comparison.answering_configuration(manifest)` (:422) and `configuration_differences(recorded, current)` (:427-446) and `guard_checkpoint_provenance(parser, provenance_path, manifest, checkpoints)` (:449-498).
- `run_comparison.CORPUS_COVERAGE = ('present','absent_topic','absent_unreleased','absent_by_design')`, `UNDETERMINED_COVERAGE='undetermined'`, `DECLARABLE_COVERAGE = CORPUS_COVERAGE + (UNDETERMINED_COVERAGE,)` (:152-154).
- `run_comparison.BASELINE_PROMPT` (:110-114): 'You are a research assistant for the {department} department of Isabela State University, Echague. Answer the following question about {department} undergraduate thesis research using only your own knowledge. Cite specific theses if you can. Question: {question}'
- `python -m evaluation.chunk_size_sweep [--dry-run] [--cached-only]` — chunk_size_sweep.py:239-310. Writes evaluation/results/chunk_size_sweep_<YYYY-MM-DD>.json.
- `chunk_size_sweep.load_corpus()`, `load_queries(papers)`, `build_variant(papers, size)`, `embed_cached(texts, cache, label, cached_only=False)`, `cosine`, `norm_of`, `score_variant(chunks, vectors, queries, query_vectors)`, exception `QuotaExhausted`.
- `python -m evaluation.summarize_jmeter RUN.jtl [RUN2.jtl ...] --output PATH --profile NAME --users N --loops N --ramp-seconds N [--require-response-code CODE ...]` — summarize_jmeter.py:85-109.
- `summarize_jmeter.summarize_rows(rows) -> {'overall': m, 'endpoints': {label: m}}` where m = {samples, average_ms, median_ms, p95_ms, p99_ms, throughput_per_second, error_rate_percent, response_codes, max_concurrent_threads, measured_run_count}.
- `summarize_jmeter.percentile(values, percent)` — linear interpolation on (n-1)*p/100, unrounded. `require_response_codes(summary, codes)` raises ValueError naming missing codes.
- `python -m evaluation.summarize_chat_load RUN.jtl [...] --output PATH --corpus STR --provider-tier STR` — summarize_chat_load.py:104-160. Profile keys are `<stem minus 'chat-'>_users`.
- `summarize_chat_load.classify(path) -> {samples, response_codes, bands, band_share_percent, answered_share_percent, answer_latency_ms{n,min,median,p95,max}, capacity_notice_latency_ms{n,max}}`; constants `CAPACITY_MAX_MS=100`, `ANSWER_MIN_MS=2000`.
- `python -m evaluation.export_sonar [--url URL] [--project KEY] --output PATH` (env SONAR_TOKEN required, SONAR_HOST_URL default http://localhost:9000, project default isu-thesis-ai-library). `build_evidence(base_url, project, token)`, `request_json(...)`, `METRICS`, `ISSUES_API_PATH`.
- `python -m evaluation.export_langsmith [--project P] [--limit N] --output PATH` (env LANGSMITH_API_KEY or settings.effective_langsmith_api_key). `safe_run_record(run)`.
- `python -m scripts.release_fingerprint [--output PATH]`; `build_manifest() -> dict`, `sha256_file(path)`, `git_commit()`, `gateway_host()`.
- `python -m scripts.export_objective2_summary` — export_objective2_summary.py:126-152. Exports `build(source=SOURCE) -> dict`; module constants SOURCE, RUN_ID='5e8fb7f21db6', RUN_DATE='2026-09-13', EVIDENCE_PATH, QUOTED_STRATUM='present', OUT, STRATUM_LABELS.
- Results JSON contract (written at run_comparison.py:941-1012): generated_at, evaluation_department, models{llm,embeddings}, evaluator{model,framework,metrics}, reproducibility{release, golden_dataset_sha256, evaluation_requirements_sha256, evaluation_script_sha256}, formal_result, dataset_validation_issues, unattempted_query_ids, rows_without_corpus_coverage, queries_scored, queries_total, rows[], ragas{baseline[],rag[]}, statistics{answer_correctness}, means{answer_correctness{baseline,rag,n}}, by_corpus_coverage{<stratum>{answer_correctness{baseline,rag,n,statistics}}}, rag_diagnostics.
- Row contract (sanitized, run_comparison.py:284-320): id, question, ground_truth, corpus_coverage, baseline_answer, baseline_latency_s, rag_answer, rag_sources[{citation_id,title}], rag_top_similarity, rag_end_to_end_latency_s, attempts, rag_kind, rag_unattempted, retrieved_context_count, retrieved_context_sha256[]; plus `failure` (first 300 chars) on a hard-failed row.

### Invariants

- The paired metric is Answer Correctness ONLY. `for metric in ('answer_correctness',)` at run_comparison.py:966 is a one-element tuple; Faithfulness and Context Precision never receive a paired test and never enter `by_corpus_coverage`. paper/build_corrections.py PROSE12 states this and cites these exact line numbers.
- A baseline arm has no retriever, therefore no retrieved contexts, therefore Context Precision is structurally undefined for it. Only the RAG arm gets Faithfulness and Context Precision — this is why the comparison is single-metric.
- Capacity and guest-budget notices are the ONLY notices that count as "never processed". `_UNATTEMPTED_NOTICES` (:89-92) holds exactly `CAPACITY_MESSAGE` and `GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE`. `NO_RELEVANT_PREFIX`, the grounded fallback and guard refusals are real system responses, are scored, and must never be retried — the three negative controls depend on it (pinned by test_evaluation_harness.py:339).
- `chat_notices.reset_capacity_limit()` runs before EVERY attempt, not just retries (:357). Without it the process-wide 60 s cooldown makes a retry an instant replay of the same notice.
- `formal_result` requires all three of: no dataset validation issues, no unattempted query ids, no rows missing corpus_coverage (:952).
- A resume is bounded by the answering configuration, not just the dataset. The run id namespaces the dataset; `<id>.provenance.json` namespaces model / route / prompt version / RAG constants / index contract / hashed source. A mismatch is refused, never silently discarded — discarding a long run is the operator's call, made with `--fresh`.
- `git_commit` is excluded from the resume comparison so a docs-only commit cannot refuse a legitimate resume; `input_sha256` already covers every source file whose content can change an answer.
- Scores and pathway rows are appended one query at a time before the next begins, so an interruption at query 38/40 resumes. A full run is ~80 answer calls + ~160 judge calls.
- `score_with_ragas` appends exactly one entry per row, in order. Every downstream pairing uses `zip(..., strict=True)` — this positional alignment is the load-bearing invariant for both the stratum split and the answers/notices split.
- Pooled `rag_diagnostics[metric]['mean']` must keep its old meaning so earlier artifacts stay comparable; the answers/notices split is additive (pinned by test_evaluation_harness.py:638).
- `answers_only.n + notices_only.n == n` for each diagnostic, because a row with `rag_kind: None` (unattempted) also has None scores and is excluded from the pooled n.
- The 95 % interval is always a two-sided Student's t interval on the mean paired difference, in both the parametric and the rank branch (:794-799). Both effect sizes are always emitted so the output shape never changes between runs.
- `mean_difference` is signed `rag - baseline`, matching the argument order given to both tests, so positive favours RAG.
- Exactly one stratum carries `quoted: true`, and it is `present` — the only stratum with a corpus-derived ground truth (export_objective2_summary.py:44, pinned by test_objective2_summary_export.py:95).
- `src/data/objective2Summary.json` must equal `build()` byte-for-byte and must be LF with a trailing newline (.gitattributes `* text=auto eol=lf`).
- `summarize_jmeter` refuses to write a summary when a `--require-response-code` was not observed; the rate-limit profile is therefore only quotable if a real 429 occurred.
- `/chat` percentiles are only ever reported over the answer band. A JTL can be 100 % HTTP 200 while answering nothing, because provider 429 is surfaced as HTTP 200 + capacity notice.
- LangSmith export is metrics-only: `safe_run_record` raises if `inputs` holds anything but `content_hidden`, or if `outputs` is truthy at all.
- Chunk-size sweep is offline by construction: it reads PDFs and the golden dataset from disk, holds every variant index in RAM, and never touches Supabase, so the frozen production index cannot be disturbed.
- Overlap is held at the production 100 tokens across every sweep variant so size and overlap are not confounded (chunk_size_sweep.py:53).
- Only the `present` stratum is scoreable for retrieval in the sweep; `absent_*` queries have no correct passage to rank (chunk_size_sweep.py:80-89).
- `evaluation/` and `scripts/` are outside both the pytest coverage scope and the CI pylint target (`routers services dependencies workers main.py config.py models.py`), so harness edits never move the 85 % gate or the 10.00/10 score.

### Data model

- golden_dataset.json — {description, validated_by_faculty_panel: bool, validation:{workflow, panel:[{name,position,date_validated}]×3, instrument_revisions}, queries:[{id:int, category, query_type, question, ground_truth, source_thesis, corpus_coverage, coverage_basis}]}. 40 queries. Categories observed: Data Mining 9, Web and Mobile Application Development 8, Network and Security 6, BSDSA 6, BSIS 4, Cross-track 3, Negative control 3, BLIS 1.
- Panel of record (2026-09-13): Dr. Benchie L. Maribao (CONTENT PANEL), Dr. Joe G. Lagarteja (TECHNICAL PANEL), Dr. Albert A. Vinluan (PANEL CHAIR).
- pathways checkpoint line — the *unsanitized* row, including `rag_context` (full prompt context) and `rag_contexts` (list of chunk bodies). 1.8 MB for 40 queries and it is PI-08 manuscript text on disk under evaluation/results/checkpoints/.
- scores checkpoint line — {id, baseline:{answer_correctness}, rag:{answer_correctness, faithfulness, context_precision}}. Reused by query id only.
- provenance.json — the nine-key `answering_configuration` projection, sorted keys, indent 2.
- build_manifest() schema_version 4 — git_commit; runtime{python, python_implementation}; models{chat,verdict,embedding}; prompt_version; generation_contract{timeout_seconds,max_retries,max_output_tokens,thinking_level}; generation_route{gateway_enabled,gateway_host,gateway_max_output_tokens,gateway_reasoning_effort,reserve_key_count}; rag_contract{chunk_size_tokens,chunk_overlap_tokens,retrieval_threshold,retrieval_match_count,retrieval_candidate_pool,retrieval_per_paper_cap,duplication_threshold,evaluation_department}; index_fingerprint{embedding_model,embedding_dimensions,preprocessing_version,chunking_version,tokenizer,chunk_size_tokens,chunk_overlap_tokens,provenance_status}; input_sha256 over requirements.txt, requirements.lock, config.py, routers/chat.py, services/prompts.py, services/retriever.py, services/question_types.py, frontend package-lock.json, both Dockerfiles, docker-compose.operations.yml.
- objective2Summary.json — {_generated_by, _do_not_edit, run{id,date,generated_at,git_commit,prompt_version,formal_result,queries_scored,queries_total,department,framework,judge_model,answer_model,metric,evidence_path}, quoted_stratum, strata:[{key,label,baseline,rag,n,delta,p_value,significant,cohens_d_z,ci_95:[lo,hi],quoted}], diagnostics{faithfulness{mean,n},context_precision{mean,n}}}.
- chunk_size_sweep result — {generated_on, corpus_papers, queries_scored, overlap_tokens, top_k, retrieval_threshold, embedding_model, embedding_dimensions, ocr_available, results:{<size>:{chunks, hit_at_1, hit_at_5, mrr, mean_top_similarity, embed_seconds}}, skipped:{<size>: reason}}. Note `hit_at_{TOP_K}` is an interpolated key name.
- summarize_chat_load report — {schema_version:1, generated_at, corpus, provider_tier, classification{capacity_notice_below_ms, answer_at_or_above_ms, basis}, caveat, profiles{<n>_users: classify()}, source_files}.
- JTL CSV columns consumed: timeStamp, elapsed, label, responseCode, success, allThreads (summarize_jmeter); timeStamp is unused by summarize_chat_load.

### Config knobs

- `LLM_BASE_URL` — must be UNSET for a formal run. A gateway is a third party and the no-training guarantee comes from Google's terms; `generation_route.gateway_enabled`/`gateway_host` in the manifest record which route served the run. Embeddings are never routed.
- `APP_ENVIRONMENT=development` — `production` forces a non-zero guest token budget via `validate_production_services`, and the harness runs as a guest.
- `GUEST_DAILY_TOKEN_BUDGET=0` — disables the budget entirely (`guest_budget.is_enabled()` is `> 0`), otherwise the evaluation is throttled into a notice.
- `GEMINI_API_KEY` — required at import time (module-level `baseline_llm`), and reused as the Ragas judge and judge-embeddings key.
- `THESIS_EVALUATION_DEPARTMENT` — the department every query is scoped to; recorded as `rag_contract.evaluation_department`. The formal run used `CCSICT`.
- `GEMINI_*` generation contract: timeout_seconds 60.0, max_retries 1, max_output_tokens 2000, thinking_level 'low' (all in the manifest; the 2000-token ceiling is what truncated three citation-repair calls in the formal run).
- Reserve keys — `reserve_key_count: 4` in the formal run. Routed for both arms deliberately, so a rate limit cannot take down only the control.
- `--dataset` (default evaluation/golden_dataset.json), `--run-id` (default dataset SHA-256[:12]), `--fresh`, `--skip-ragas`, `--allow-unvalidated`.
- `evaluation.run_comparison._EVALUATOR_MAX_TOKENS = 16384` — not a measurement parameter; sized to clear instructor's truncation point for the longest baseline answer a 40-query run can produce.
- `chunk_size_sweep`: `CHUNK_SIZES=[400,600,800,1000,1200]`, `OVERLAP=100`, `_REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE=90`, `_SLICE=15`, cache at `<repo>/tmp/chunk_sweep_embeddings.jsonl` keyed on `model:dimensions:text`; `--dry-run`, `--cached-only`.
- `summarize_chat_load`: `CAPACITY_MAX_MS=100`, `ANSWER_MIN_MS=2000`; CLI `--corpus` and `--provider-tier` are required free-text provenance labels.
- JMeter `-J` properties: HOST (127.0.0.1), PORT (8000), USERS, LOOPS, RAMP, plus RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_MS (60000) and QUESTION on chat_load.jmx.
- `SONAR_HOST_URL` / `SONAR_TOKEN`; `LANGSMITH_PROJECT` / `LANGSMITH_API_KEY` (falling back to settings.effective_*).

### Error handling

- Dataset invalid → `parser.error` (exit 2) listing every condition, unless `--allow-unvalidated` downgrades it to a printed WARNING.
- Checkpoint exists with no provenance file → `parser.error` telling the operator to use `--fresh`.
- Checkpoint exists with a changed configuration → `parser.error` naming each changed key, `input_sha256` expanded to individual file paths so the message is actionable.
- A raised exception in either arm → up to 4 attempts with 20/45/90 s backoff; on exhaustion the query becomes an `_unattempted_row` carrying `failure` (first 300 chars of str(error)) and the run continues rather than dying.
- A capacity or guest-budget notice from the RAG arm → same 4-attempt ladder with the cooldown cleared first; on exhaustion the last observed pair is recorded with `rag_unattempted: True`.
- Any unattempted row → excluded from scoring, excluded from the paired statistics, listed in `unattempted_query_ids`, printed as a WARNING, and forces `formal_result: false`.
- A resumed row with no `corpus_coverage` → listed in `rows_without_corpus_coverage`, printed as a WARNING, forces `formal_result: false`. Only reachable with an explicit `--run-id`.
- A judge call failure → `judged()` retries on the same 20/45/90 ladder and re-raises on the fourth, which aborts scoring. Scores already checkpointed survive.
- Statistical test withheld in exactly two cases: fewer than 3 pairs, or all paired differences identical — each returns a `{'note': ...}` dict with no statistics keys at all (pinned by test_evaluation_harness.py:580).
- Non-finite or None metric values drop their pair from the means and the tests, so `means.n` can legitimately be smaller than `queries_total`.
- `pandas` ImportError → CSV silently skipped, JSON still written.
- `summarize_jmeter` missing run file → not handled; `summarize_chat_load` prints `missing run file: <path>` and returns exit 1.
- `summarize_jmeter.require_response_codes` raises ValueError before the output file is written.
- `export_sonar` with no SONAR_TOKEN → `SystemExit('SONAR_TOKEN is required')`. `export_langsmith` with no key → `SystemExit('LANGSMITH_API_KEY is required')`; a run carrying payload → `ValueError` naming the run id.
- `chunk_size_sweep` missing corpus PDF → FileNotFoundError; a variant needing uncached vectors under `--cached-only` → `QuotaExhausted`, recorded per size in the result file's `skipped` map and the run continues.
- `export_objective2_summary` missing source run or missing `<RUN_ID>.provenance.json` → prints to stderr and returns 1, so a wrong RUN_ID cannot put a plausible-but-false provenance string on screen.

### Tests that pin it

- tests/test_evaluation_harness.py — validate_formal_dataset acceptance and every rejection path (missing stratum, `undetermined`, placeholders, incomplete or non-distinct panel, non-ISO dates); `TestGoldenDatasetStrata` pins the shipped 16/17/4/3 counts, requires a `coverage_basis` on every query, asserts negative controls are exactly `absent_by_design`, asserts an `absent_unreleased` query names an unreleased track in its own wording, and that `validation.instrument_revisions` covers every restratified query; `_ranked_contexts` order restoration; `sanitize_evaluation_rows` never exporting context; that only outage notices count as unattempted; capacity-notice retry recovery; persistent outage flagged not scored; a no-relevant answer never retried; the cooldown cleared on retry; checkpoint write/resume and JSON-lines round trip; the commit alone not invalidating a checkpoint; a changed input named per file; provenance recorded on creation, unchanged config resuming, changed model refusing, missing provenance refusing; a persistent provider error excluded rather than fatal; the effect size / interval values computed by hand; rank-biserial bounds and sign; the no-test note carrying no statistics; the statistical contract's key set; the diagnostics answers/notices split and the pooled figure's unchanged meaning; `rag_kind` classified from the response object. scipy-dependent tests skip via `importlib.util.find_spec`.
- tests/test_objective2_summary_export.py — the committed summary equals `build()`; LF + trailing newline; run id / date / formal_result / 40 of 40 / ragas==0.4.3; pooled 0.2169→0.3024, p≈3.222e-05, significant, n=40; the quoted stratum is `present`, n=16, delta 0.0601, p 0.1257, NOT significant, CI straddling zero; exactly one quoted row; five strata each with a bool flag, n>=1 and a human label; `absent_unreleased` n=4 and `absent_by_design` n=3 still exported.
- tests/test_jmeter_summary.py — percentile interpolation and empty-input zero; average/median/error rate/max_concurrent_threads/response_codes; `require_response_codes` raising on a missing code; throughput denominator excluding idle time between distinct runs.
- tests/test_evaluation_exports.py — LangSmith export carrying metrics but no payload, and rejecting a run whose inputs hold real content; Sonar export reading hotspots from the component measure and never calling /api/hotspots/search.
- tests/test_readme_accuracy.py — pins README.md, rag-thesis-frontend/README.md, jmeter/README.md and evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md facts against the code.
- tests/test_release_fingerprint.py — pins the manifest shape and inputs.
- tests/test_corpus_manifest.py — pins `EXPECTED_PAPER_COUNT = 12`.

### Gotchas

- **The two arms run concurrently against the same provider, and the harness reports their latencies as if isolated.** `_attempt_pathways` (:277) `asyncio.gather`s baseline and RAG. The formal run's `baseline_latency_s` (mean 12.49 s, max 35.0 s) and `rag_end_to_end_latency_s` (mean 16.0 s, max 64.4 s) were therefore measured under self-inflicted two-way concurrency and event-loop contention. Nothing in the results file or the evidence ledger says so.
- **`asyncio.gather` without `return_exceptions` does not cancel the sibling.** If the baseline raises, the RAG coroutine keeps running to completion and its result is discarded, spending a full retrieval + generation + citation-validation cycle of quota for nothing — then `_run_query` sleeps 20-90 s and does it again.
- **`--skip-ragas` still writes `formal_result: true`.** The flag is not consulted at :952, so a valid dataset plus `--skip-ragas` produces a file that declares itself a formal result while containing no `ragas`, no `statistics`, no `means` and no `rag_diagnostics`. `queries_scored` is also computed as `len(rows) - len(unattempted)` before scoring, so it reports 40 "scored" with zero scores.
- **`--allow-unvalidated`'s help text overstates what it does.** It says "mark output non-formal", but it only suppresses the abort; `formal_result` is driven by `dataset_issues` being non-empty. A valid dataset run with the flag set is still stamped formal.
- **`json.dumps(..., allow_nan=False)` at :1015 can destroy a completed run's output.** `output['ragas']` carries the raw metric values; only the statistics and diagnostics paths filter with `math.isfinite`. A single NaN from a judge call raises ValueError *after* every answer and every score has been paid for, and no results file is written. The checkpoints survive, so a re-run recovers — but the failure lands at the very end of a multi-hour run.
- **Ragas score reuse is keyed on query id, not on the answer text scored.** Stated as an open gap in iso25010_evidence.md:246-252. Deleting only the pathways checkpoint while keeping the scores checkpoint would attach old scores to new answers; `--fresh` deletes both, but nothing enforces that pairing.
- **`evaluation/run_comparison.py` is itself outside the resume guard's compared set.** Its digest is published as `reproducibility.evaluation_script_sha256` but is not in `_ANSWERING_CONFIGURATION_KEYS`, so editing the harness and resuming is the same class of defect as F10, one level up. Same for `services/citations.py`, which decides what attribution reaches the reader but is not a `build_manifest` input — documented at iso25010_evidence.md:167-176 and deliberately not fixed.
- **A changed corpus behind an unchanged index contract fingerprints identically.** `index_fingerprint` is the contract (embedding model, dimensions, chunking/preprocessing versions), not an inventory. Re-ingesting different manuscripts still needs `--fresh` and nothing detects it.
- **`guard_checkpoint_provenance` silently rewrites provenance when no checkpoint file exists.** A run that dies before the first row is appended leaves a provenance file with zero recorded answers; the next run sees `resumable == []` and overwrites it with whatever the configuration now is.
- **Retry is only partly lossless.** If attempts 1-3 return capacity notices and attempt 4 *raises*, `_run_query` returns `_unattempted_row` and throws away the three recorded notice responses.
- **`evaluation_trace` is populated only inside the retrieval block** (routers/chat.py:2615). Any query that takes a conversational fast path, is refused by `services/guards`, or short-circuits on capacity/guest budget produces `rag_contexts == []`, which silently skips Faithfulness and Context Precision for that row (they become None and are counted in `not_applicable`). In the formal run all 40 rows retrieved 5 blocks, so this never fired — including for the three negative controls, which is why `notices_only` has n=8 with faithfulness 0.20.
- **The committed CSV and JSON still contain manuscript-derived text.** `sanitize_evaluation_rows` strips retrieved chunk bodies only. `ground_truth` (drafted from PI-08 manuscripts), `rag_answer` (which quotes them) and `baseline_answer` all remain, and `comparison_20260913_064053.csv` is 264 KB in git. The unsanitized `5e8fb7f21db6.pathways.jsonl` (1.8 MB of raw context) sits in `evaluation/results/checkpoints/`.
- **The 20-user JMeter profiles never achieved 20 concurrent users.** Measured from the JTLs: `provider_run_1/2.jtl` have `allThreads == 1` on all 300 samples, `provider_run_3.jtl` peaks at 2. With a 30 s ramp over 20 threads, a thread starts every 1.5 s and its 5 loops × 3 sub-second GETs finish well inside that window. The headline "900/900, p95 204 ms" quoted at iso25010_evidence.md:1126 is a serial latency figure, not a concurrency figure. `summarize_jmeter` does report `max_concurrent_threads: 2`, but the evidence prose never uses it.
- **Same problem on the /chat profiles, for the opposite reason.** `chat-10.jtl` and `chat-20.jtl` both have `allThreads == 1` throughout: capacity notices return in 1-31 ms, so threads never overlap. The claim "at 10 and 20 concurrent users it served 60 requests in 14 seconds at 4.2 req/s" describes 60 sequential 1-30 ms notices.
- **`rate_limit_test.jmx` sends no `X-Guest-ID`.** All 20 threads therefore share whatever key the limiter falls back to (IP), which is what produces the clean 30/30 split against `rate_limit_chat = '30/minute'` (config.py:94). Its JTL also shows `allThreads == 1` throughout, so the "burst" is serial. The summary reports `error_rate_percent: 50` because a 429 sets `success=false` — the intended result reads as a 50 % failure rate.
- **The chunk-size sweep does not reproduce production chunking.** `build_variant` splits `ExtractedDocument.text`, which joins pages with `'\n\n'` (services/document_processor.py:63). Production uses `split_document`, which joins pages with a single space specifically because "newlines are strong recursive-split separators, so inserting one here would make a physical page break act like a semantic section boundary" (services/chunker.py:263-266). The 800-token variant in the sweep is therefore not the 800-token chunking that is deployed.
- **The sweep embeds queries with the document task type.** `embed_cached([q['question'] ...])` → `embed_texts` → `client.embed_documents`. Production embeds a search query with `embed_text` → `client.embed_query` (services/embedder.py:38-43, services/retriever.py:783). Different task type, different vector.
- **The sweep scores pure cosine top-k, not the deployed selection stage.** `score_variant` (chunk_size_sweep.py:196) takes the global top-5 above 0.30. Production runs a 15-candidate pool → deterministic hybrid rerank (lexical + section score) → per-paper cap of 3 → 5 blocks → `long_context_reorder` (services/retriever.py:785-869). The module docstring's claim that it "scores the pipeline as deployed" is not accurate for the selection stage.
- **The sweep result does not support "800 is optimal", and the paper now says so — but says something the sweep did not compute.** `chunk_size_sweep_2026-09-14.json`: 400 and 600 both hit@1 50.00 / hit@5 75.00; 800 and 1000 both 43.75 / 68.75; MRR peaks at 600 (0.6042) and is lowest at 800 (0.5333). 1200 was skipped on quota. The corrected paper text (paper/build_corrections.py:129-137) says retrieval is "statistically indistinguishable across 400 to 1,000 tokens" — no statistical test exists anywhere in `chunk_size_sweep.py`; it emits point estimates over 16 queries with no interval and no test.
- **The sweep ran with OCR unavailable.** `ocr_available: false` in the result file. `ExtractedDocument.unresolved_scanned_pages` exists and carries the exact page list, but the sweep ignores it and records only the boolean. The paper correction says 94 scanned pages were excluded; the artifact does not contain that number.
- **`export_langsmith.safe_run_record` always reports `error_category: 'str'` for a real error.** Line 27 computes `type(run.error).__name__`; LangSmith's `Run.error` is a string field, so the type name of the value is `str`, never an exception class. The one test covers `error=None` only. A single run with a truthy `outputs` also aborts the entire export, because `safe_run_record` raises inside a list comprehension.
- **`summarize_chat_load` has no `require_response_code` equivalent.** It will happily write an evidence file whose profiles show `answered_share_percent: 0.0`, which is exactly what `chat_rag_load_report.json` does for the 10- and 20-user profiles.
- **`summarize_chat_load` classifies from `responseCode` and `elapsed` only** — it never reads the `success` column, and the 100 ms / 2 000 ms thresholds were calibrated on one 2026-08-04 session against a synthetic 10-thesis corpus on the free tier. A 502 that takes 26 s (two such samples in `chat-2.jtl`) is banded as `failure` but its latency is silently dropped from every reported figure.
- **`summarize_jmeter.percentile` returns an unrounded value; `summarize_chat_load.percentile` rounds only in the interpolated branch and returns an unrounded float on an exact index.** Cosmetic, but the two summarizers disagree on the same computation.
- **Profiles run back to back share one depleting quota.** The four `/chat` profiles are explicitly not independent samples — the 2-user profile looks worse than the 5-user profile only because it ran later. Both the jmeter README and iso25010_evidence.md warn against reading them as a concurrency curve; the report JSON itself carries no such flag per profile.
- **`export_objective2_summary.py` hardcodes SOURCE, RUN_ID, RUN_DATE and EVIDENCE_PATH.** Deliberate — the results directory holds seven earlier exploratory runs and "latest file" would swap the paper's result for a rehearsal — but it means a legitimate re-run requires editing the script, the test's expected figures, and Section 3.2.5 together.
- **`export_objective2_summary.build()` iterates `run['by_corpus_coverage']` in JSON insertion order** and falls back to `STRATUM_LABELS.get(key, key)`. A new stratum with no label entry produces `label == key`, which `test_every_stratum_carries_its_own_significance_flag` catches only because it asserts `label != key`.
- **Three of the four smallest strata reach significance and the one the paper quotes does not.** pooled p=3.222e-05 (n=40), absent_topic p=0.0009 (n=17), absent_unreleased p=0.0419 (n=4), absent_by_design p=0.0122 (n=3), present p=0.1257 (n=16). No family-wise or FDR correction exists anywhere; `significant_at_0.05` is the sole decision rule. Shapiro-Wilk selected the parametric branch for every one of the five, including n=3 and n=4 where the test has essentially no power to reject normality.
- **Judge and candidate are the same model family, and the judge shares the retriever's embedding space.** Judge `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`, candidate `gemini-3.6-flash`, Answer Correctness' semantic component embedded with `models/gemini-embedding-001` — the same model the index uses. Disclosed in the paper (P7-4) as a limitation of the automated scores; no human-scored subsample exists.
- **A published figure in the evidence ledger disagrees with the JSON it came from.** iso25010_evidence.md:37-41 records it rather than fixing it: the table prints `absent_unreleased` 0.1990→0.3600 and `absent_by_design` 0.2622→0.3761 where the results JSON has 0.1991→0.3601 and 0.2616→0.3755. The JSON is authoritative; `objective2Summary.json` matches the JSON, not the table.
- **`run_comparison.py` writes CRLF on Windows while `.gitattributes` stores LF**, so a SHA-256 taken from the working tree does not verify against any checkout. The evidence hashes at iso25010_evidence.md:117-123 are of the committed files and say so explicitly.
- **`evaluation/*.py` IS analysed by SonarQube but is NOT linted by pylint or covered by pytest.** `sonar.exclusions` excludes only `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/**` (sonar-project.properties:42), while the CI lint and coverage targets name only `routers services dependencies workers main.py config.py models.py`.
- **`baseline_llm` is constructed at module import** (run_comparison.py:101), so importing the module — which `tests/test_evaluation_harness.py` does at collection — instantiates a `ChatGoogleGenerativeAI` and requires `settings.gemini_api_key` to exist.
- **`dataset['queries']` is read before validation** (:861), so a malformed dataset raises KeyError instead of producing the curated `parser.error` list.
- **`scripts/export_objective2_summary.py`, `tests/test_objective2_summary_export.py` and `rag-thesis-frontend/src/data/` are all untracked in git** as of this reading, while `AdminOverview.jsx` (modified, uncommitted) imports the generated JSON. The frontend currently does not build from a clean checkout.
- **`evaluation/results/reindex_dry_run.json` is UTF-16LE with a BOM** — a stale PowerShell redirect artifact, unreadable by `json.load` with the default encoding, still listed in the artifact-hash table.
- **`thesis_load_test.jmx` ships a hardcoded `paste-a-valid-supabase-jwt-here` placeholder** and an embedded ResultCollector writing to a relative `results/summary.csv`. Retained as superseded; both the README and jmeter/README say not to use it for new evidence, but nothing disables it.

### Open questions

- Context Precision at 0.1944 pooled (0.2221 answers-only) is low independently of the notice rows and is explicitly left open in iso25010_evidence.md:119-120. It is a retrieval-ranking diagnostic outside the paired claim, but nothing in the repository explains it.
- Eight of the sixteen `present` queries (ids 6, 7, 14, 15, 20, 32, 33, 36) returned the grounded fallback despite retrieving five blocks at top similarity 0.67-0.74. Five failed `enforce_citation_coverage` and three hit `TruncatedGeneration` at the 2 000-token ceiling during citation repair. This is the single largest lever on the figure Section 3.2.5 quotes, and it is deliberately not acted on because fixing it is a `PROMPT_VERSION` bump and a full re-run.
- Whether `services/citations.py` and `evaluation/run_comparison.py` should join `build_manifest`'s hashed input set. Both decide what a reported result says and neither is covered; widening the set is a schema_version bump.
- Whether the chunk-size sweep should be re-run with production `split_document` chunking, `embed_query` for questions, the real selection stage, OCR available, and the 1 200-token variant completed — and whether the paper's "statistically indistinguishable" wording can stand without a test having been computed.
- No family-wise or FDR correction across the five simultaneous tests on one dataset. The paper designates `present` as the pre-registered claim and everything else as secondary, but the artifact flags all four absent strata as `significant: true` with no correction noted.
- No human-scored subsample anchoring the same-family automated judge. Disclosed as open in paper/build_corrections.py P7-4.
- The JMeter evidence has never been re-measured with genuine concurrency, on a paid provider tier, against the approved corpus, with independent quota windows per profile — all four are listed as required before the formal evaluation at iso25010_evidence.md:1133-1137 and none has happened.
- The corpus lock, its receipt and the four PI-08 institutional approvals still do not exist; `corpus_manifest.template.json` has all four `approvals` fields null. The panel sign-off validates the instrument, not the corpus release.
- `scripts/export_objective2_summary.py`, its test, and `rag-thesis-frontend/src/data/` are untracked while `AdminOverview.jsx` imports the generated JSON — the frontend does not currently build from a clean checkout.

**Depends on:** routers.chat._chat_impl (the RAG arm is the real production function, called with request=None, user=None — which bypasses the Turnstile check at chat.py:2016 and both @limiter.limit decorators at chat.py:2008-2009, since those live on the route wrapper), services.chat_notices (CAPACITY_MESSAGE, GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE, KIND_ANSWER, KIND_NOTICE, response_kind, reset_capacity_limit), services.gemini_pool (arun, CHAT, gateway_enabled), services.index_provenance.current_index_fingerprint / PREPROCESSING_VERSION, services.prompts.PROMPT_VERSION, services.retriever (indirectly: search_chunks assigns citation ids before long_context_reorder, which is what makes _ranked_contexts sound), services.chunker (TOKENIZER_ENCODING, _TOKEN_SEPARATORS, count_tokens — the sweep imports a private symbol), services.document_processor (extract_pdf_document, tesserocr), services.embedder.embed_texts, scripts.release_fingerprint (build_manifest, sha256_file), config.settings (gemini_chat_model, gemini_verdict_model, gemini_embed_model, gemini_timeout_seconds, gemini_max_retries, gemini_max_output_tokens, gemini_thinking_level, thesis_evaluation_department, retrieval_match_count, retrieval_threshold, embedding_dimensions, guest_daily_token_budget), warning_filters.silence_known_third_party_warnings (must run before the first langchain* import — forces the unusual import order at run_comparison.py:57-66), evaluation extras: ragas 0.4.3 (metrics.collections AnswerCorrectness/Faithfulness/ContextPrecision, llm_factory, GoogleEmbeddings), openai AsyncOpenAI, google.genai, scipy.stats, pandas, External: Gemini OpenAI-compatible endpoint, SonarQube web API, LangSmith API, Apache JMeter 5.6.3 on OpenJDK 25

**Used by:** paper/build_corrections.py — PROSE1 (P1-5 metric semantics), PROSE7 (P7-4 same-family judge, P7-5 notices rule and effect size), PROSE8 (P8-1 which metrics use the reference), PROSE10 (P10-3 the four coverage strata), PROSE12 (3.2.5 statistical treatment, which cites run_comparison.py line numbers directly), and the chunk-size claim at :129-137, rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminOverview.jsx (via src/data/objective2Summary.json), docs/deck/build.py — scrapes Objective 4 figures out of iso25010_evidence.md by regex, so a figure the ledger does not contain cannot reach a slide, docs/evidence/OBJECTIVE_2_COMPARISON_2026-09-13.html — the standalone Material-3 evidence page the admin card links to, tests/test_readme_accuracy.py — pins README.md, jmeter/README.md and evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md against the code, tests/test_evaluation_harness.py, tests/test_jmeter_summary.py, tests/test_evaluation_exports.py, tests/test_objective2_summary_export.py, tests/test_release_fingerprint.py, tests/test_corpus_manifest.py

**Adversarial fact-check:** verdict `mostly-accurate`, 14 correction(s), 6 omission(s). Corrections are already folded into the text above. They are kept because each records a wrong belief a competent reader reached from the same files - the same trap is available to you.

- **Wrong:** "In the formal run all 40 rows retrieved 5 blocks, so this never fired — including for the three negative controls, which is why `notices_only` has n=8 with faithfulness 0.20." (and the invariant that the three negative controls score ~0 on both diagnostics while carrying contexts)
  **Actual:** The eight `notices_only` rows are NOT the negative controls. All eight (ids 6, 7, 14, 15, 20, 32, 33, 36) are `corpus_coverage: present` rows carrying the grounded retrieval fallback. The three negative controls (ids 38, 39, 40, `absent_by_design`) are classified `rag_kind: 'answer'` — each returned a model-written prose absence statement ("The retrieved studies in the CCSICT archive do not cover quantum computing…") with 5 sources, which `chat_notices.response_kind` does not match against `NO_RELEVANT_PREFIX` or `GROUNDED_FALLBACK_PREFIX`, so they sit in `answers_only` (n=32). The scenario `summarize_rag_diagnostics`' own docstring predicts ("The three negative controls do exactly this by design") did not occur in the formal run. (`rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:719-721; services/chat_notices.py:254-269; evaluation/results/comparison_20260913_064053.json (rows 38-40 rag_kind='answer'; rows 6,7,14,15,20,32,33,36 rag_kind='notice', all corpus_coverage='present')`)
- **Wrong:** "The committed CSV and JSON still contain manuscript-derived text … The unsanitized `5e8fb7f21db6.pathways.jsonl` (1.8 MB of raw context) sits in `evaluation/results/checkpoints/`." — presented as a committed PI-08 exposure alongside the CSV.
  **Actual:** `evaluation/results/` is gitignored wholesale. `git check-ignore` confirms `checkpoints/5e8fb7f21db6.provenance.json`, `comparison_20260912_124741.json`, `chunk_size_sweep_2026-09-14.json` and `reindex_dry_run.json` are all ignored by that one rule. Only four files under `results/` were force-added: `comparison_20260728_140718.json`, `comparison_20260913_064053.json`, `comparison_20260913_064053.csv`, plus the `jmeter/` subdirectory. The 1.8 MB pathways checkpoint exists on disk only and is not in the repository. The claim about the committed CSV/JSON still carrying ground_truth and rag_answer is correct; the checkpoint claim is not a committed exposure. (`rag-thesis-backend/.gitignore:23 (`evaluation/results/`); `git ls-files rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/``)
- **Wrong:** The untracked-file problem is scoped to "`scripts/export_objective2_summary.py`, its test, and `rag-thesis-frontend/src/data/` are untracked while `AdminOverview.jsx` imports the generated JSON — the frontend does not currently build from a clean checkout."
  **Actual:** It is worse than that and the map missed the mechanism. `main()` refuses to export unless `results/checkpoints/5e8fb7f21db6.provenance.json` exists, and that file is gitignored — so on a clean checkout the generator cannot regenerate the file it is the sole authority for, even with the script tracked. Separately, `tests/test_objective2_summary_export.py:30-33` is `pytestmark = skipif(not SOURCE.exists() or not OUT.exists())`, so on a clean checkout (OUT absent) the entire pin silently skips rather than failing. The 'regenerating from a different run breaks the build' guarantee holds only on a working tree that already has the ignored artifacts. (`rag-thesis-backend/scripts/export_objective2_summary.py:132-135; rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_objective2_summary_export.py:30-33; rag-thesis-backend/.gitignore:23`)

The only byte-level assertions are `b'\r' not in raw` and `raw.endswith(b'\n')` in a separate test. (`rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_objective2_summary_export.py:36-38, :45-50, :53-57`)

The shipped `evaluation/dev_smoke_dataset.json` carries no `corpus_coverage` key on any of its three queries (query keys are exactly id/question/ground_truth/source_thesis), so a plain `python -m evaluation.run_comparison --dataset evaluation/dev_smoke_dataset.json --allow-unvalidated` hits the `uncovered` branch on a fresh run under the default run id `e9591c80b9f1`. No `--run-id` and no stale checkpoint required. (`rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:906-921 (the comment is wrong); evaluation/dev_smoke_dataset.json`)

The map inherited the off-by-one from the script's own comment at export_objective2_summary.py:35, which says the same thing and is also wrong. That is a second enforcement point for the positional-alignment invariant the map correctly identified in run_comparison. (`docs/deck/evidence.py:24, :120-163, :202-232`)
- **Wrong:** The RAG arm is scoped by `department_filter=settings.thesis_evaluation_department` — "`THESIS_EVALUATION_DEPARTMENT` — the department every query is scoped to".

The scoping is correct but comes from the guest branch, not from the field the harness sets; a harness run could not be pointed at another department through it. (`rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py:105-108; routers/chat.py:2112-2114; evaluation/run_comparison.py:272-278`)
- **Wrong:** "`summarize_jmeter.percentile` returns an unrounded value; `summarize_chat_load.percentile` rounds only in the interpolated branch … Cosmetic, but the two summarizers disagree on the same computation."
  **Actual:** The function-level description is right but the conclusion is wrong: every `summarize_jmeter` caller wraps the result in `round(..., 2)`, so the emitted `median_ms`/`p95_ms`/`p99_ms` are 2dp exactly as `summarize_chat_load`'s interpolated branch is. The two tools do not disagree in their output. (`rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/summarize_jmeter.py:12-21, :46-48; evaluation/summarize_chat_load.py:47-56`)
- **Wrong:** "all four are listed as required before the formal evaluation at iso25010_evidence.md:1133-1137" (genuine concurrency, paid tier, approved corpus, independent quota windows).

- **Wrong:** `statistical_treatment` and the rest of the file use `zip(..., strict=True)` as the load-bearing pairing discipline.
  **Actual:** `statistical_treatment` is the exception: `diffs = [r - b for r, b in zip(rag_scores, baseline_scores)]` at :773 has no `strict=True`, unlike :708-709, :735, :972-974 and :1004. The length guard at :771 only rejects each list being shorter than 3, so mismatched-length inputs would silently truncate to the shorter one rather than raise. Not reachable from `main` (the pairs are built together), but it is a real inconsistency in a public function the tests call directly. (`rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:771-775`)
- **Wrong:** "LangSmith export is metrics-only: `safe_run_record` raises if `inputs` holds anything but `content_hidden`, or if `outputs` is truthy at all."
  **Actual:** Accurate as written but the guarantee is weaker than the invariant implies: the check is `set(inputs) - {'content_hidden'}`, i.e. on key names only. A run whose `inputs` is `{'content_hidden': '<the actual user question>'}` passes the guard silently — the value is never inspected, and it is dropped from the record rather than raising. The privacy guard is structural about key names, not about payload content. (`rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/export_langsmith.py:11-14`)
- **Wrong:** "paper/build_corrections.py PROSE12 states this and cites these exact line numbers" (implying the paper builder's harness anchors are current).

Within PROSE12's comment itself: `statistical_treatment` is :761-824 not :761-812, the pooled call is at :983 not :987, and `significant_at_0.05` is at :804 not :806. The substantive claims (single-element metric tuple at :966, per-stratum call at :1005, CI outside the branch at :797, two withholding reasons at :772/:774, no FDR correction) all check out. (`paper/build_corrections.py:432, :785-798; evaluation/run_comparison.py:404-419, :579-597, :761-824, :983`)
- **Wrong:** Assorted line anchors in the map.
  **Actual:** Off by a few in several places: `_chat_impl` is routers/chat.py:2076 not :2075, and it is a 5-line wrapper whose only job is `response.kind = chat_notices.response_kind(response)` — the pipeline and the `evaluation_trace` fill live in `_chat_impl_unstamped` at :2098, trace at :2615-2622. `dataset['queries']` is read at run_comparison.py:864 not :861. `sanitize_evaluation_rows` is :223-239; `summarize_rag_diagnostics` :714-758; `_score_with_ragas` :561-686; `is_unattempted` :95-98. In the ledger: the results-vs-table discrepancy is :35-40 not :37-41; the Context Precision 'left open' note is :105-106 not :119-120; the Ragas score-reuse gap is :237-238 not :246-252; the '900/900, p95 204 ms' line is :1124 not :1126. jmeter/README.md is 80 lines not 81; objective2Summary.json is 113 lines; the AdminOverview block runs 200-285 (table 218-258, caveat 264-272). (`multiple`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- **`chunk_size_sweep_2026-09-14.json` is gitignored and untracked**, yet `paper/build_corrections.py:120-128` cites it by name as the ground truth for the corrected chunk-size prose ("1,200 is absent because the run stopped on the embedding tier's daily quota, and the corpus's 94 scanned pages are excluded because the measuring host had no OCR runtime -- both recorded in evaluation/results/chunk_size_sweep_2026-09-14.json"). Neither fact is verifiable from the repository: the artifact is not committed, and even on disk it records only `ocr_available: false` and `skipped: {"1200": "size 1200: 98 of 192 vectors not cached"}` — the 94-page number appears nowhere in it. The map caught the 94-page half; it missed that the whole artifact is absent from git. (rag-thesis-backend/.gitignore:23)
- The map's `--fresh` account is complete for the three files it names, but **`--fresh` does not touch `evaluation/results/comparison_*.json|csv`**, which are timestamped per run, so repeated runs accumulate result files that only `SOURCE` pinning in export_objective2_summary.py distinguishes. That is the actual reason the pin exists, stated once in the script comment and nowhere in the README.
- `build_manifest()` calls `current_index_fingerprint()` (scripts/release_fingerprint.py:137), which is a live index-provenance lookup — so `main()` at run_comparison.py:898 performs a Supabase-dependent call before any query runs, and the formal run recorded `provenance_status: "verified"` from it. The map lists `index_fingerprint` as a manifest field but never notes that building the manifest is not a pure local operation.
- The formal run's manifest records `runtime.python: "3.14.6"` while CLAUDE.md states the Dockerfile asserts Python is exactly 3.14.7. Since `runtime` is one of the nine `_ANSWERING_CONFIGURATION_KEYS`, a patch-version bump on the measuring host alone is enough to refuse a resume of the formal checkpoint — a stricter consequence than the map's "models, route, prompt, RAG constants, hashed source" framing suggests. (evaluation/results/checkpoints/5e8fb7f21db6.provenance.json; run_comparison.py:409-419)
- `chat-5.jtl` reached `allThreads == 5` and `chat-2.jtl` reached 2, so the /chat concurrency criticism is specific to the 10- and 20-user profiles (both flat at 1). The map's blanket "Same problem on the /chat profiles" overstates it slightly; the two profiles that actually answered anything did overlap threads.
- `_run_pathways` resumes by `row['id']` from the pathways checkpoint but never verifies the checkpointed `question` still matches the dataset's. The provenance guard covers configuration, the run id covers the dataset file's digest — but with an explicit `--run-id` a reworded question replays the old answer. This is the same class as the score-reuse gap the ledger records at :237-238, one level up, and neither the ledger nor the map states it for pathways. (run_comparison.py:392-401, :510-524)

## 3. `be-tests` - Backend test suite - what is actually pinned

54 test modules plus `conftest.py` that collect 2,143 tests and run in ~37 seconds with zero real I/O (measured: `2140 passed, 3 skipped in 36.62s` on the repo's `.venv`). The suite has two distinct halves: behavioural unit tests that drive routers/services through ~45 hand-written Supabase/LLM stub classes, and "repository-fact" tests that read committed SQL, Markdown, Dockerfiles, JSON evidence and PowerShell scripts as text and assert literal strings in them. The second half is what freezes the thesis artifact: model names, RAG constants, migration guarantees, notice wording, statistical figures and the OpenAI schema are pinned as exact values so a "small" code change also fails as a paper change. CI runs the whole thing with `--cov-fail-under=85` over `routers services dependencies workers main config models` only.

### Key files

- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/conftest.py` (88 lines) - The only shared bootstrap. Inserts the backend root on sys.path (:13), calls warning_filters.silence_known_third_party_warnings() BEFORE any langchain import (:15-17), then force-writes 17 env vars into os.environ (:19-44) so no developer .env leaks in. Comment at :21-25 records the two real incidents: four live GEMINI_API_KEYS reddened rotation assertions locally, and a set LLM_BASE_URL put the gateway in front of every monkeypatched chat.llm so twelve tests made real network calls. :47-88 prints a WARNING (never fails) when sys.prefix is not <backend>/.venv, because pytest.ini names starlette.exceptions.StarletteDeprecationWarning as a filter category and resolves it by import at config-parse time — a wrong interpreter aborts collection with AttributeError before one test runs.
- `rag-thesis-backend/pytest.ini` (9 lines) - testpaths=tests, pythonpath=., addopts=-v --tb=short, two filterwarnings entries (one of which is the Starlette class that makes the wrong-interpreter failure look like a library bug), and one declared marker: `integration`. CI passes no `-m` filter, so integration tests are collected and self-skip rather than being deselected.
- `rag-thesis-backend/.coveragerc` (7 lines) - branch=false, precision=2. Branch coverage is OFF, so the 85% gate is line coverage only.
- `.github/workflows/quality.yml` (~340 lines) - quality.yml:47 pins python-version '3.14.7'; :70-72 is the coverage command (scripts/ and evaluation/ are NOT in it); :75 pylint over the same module list.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_guards_multilingual.py` (364 lines) - 323 tests — the single largest file by test count. Two frozen string corpora: MUST_REFUSE (~100 authoring requests in Tagalog/Ilocano/Taglish/English) and MUST_ALLOW (~180 legitimate retrieval questions), each parametrized against services.guards.prohibited_reason, plus a disjointness check (:363).
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_chat_routing_multilingual.py` (421 lines) - 234 tests. Parametrized corpora for eight routers/chat.py private predicates: _is_archive_inventory_question, _is_archive_count_question, _is_archive_continuation_question, _extract_author_name, _is_ambiguous_system_identity_question, _is_ambiguous_system_origin_question, _is_self_platform_reference, _is_system_origin_question. Each corpus is paired with a LOOKALIKES corpus that must NOT match.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_followup_multilingual.py` (218 lines) - 162 tests. MUST_FOLLOW_UP (42) vs MUST_NOT_PIN (112) against services.guards.is_ambiguous_followup, plus TestTheProgramNamedIT pinning that the BSIT acronym 'IT' is not read as the pronoun 'it'.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_chat_logic.py` (614 lines) - 119 tests over routers.chat pure helpers: citation filtering, the five conversational fast paths, archive-inventory/count/continuation responses and their pagination strings, author-lookup answer shaping, system-provenance replies, bare-title-fragment capture, Filipino/Ilocano greeting routing, and the PYTHONHASHSEED determinism fix (_GREETINGS_BY_LENGTH longest-first).
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_rag_controls.py` (584 lines) - 114 tests. The guard/follow-up/citation/config quadrant: prohibited_reason corpora, is_ambiguous_followup false-positive corpus, validate_citations + enforce_citation_coverage + normalize_citation_markers exact-output assertions, Settings validation bounds, and department resolution.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_router_error_paths.py` (1,274 lines) - 101 tests. Error branches of chat.py and upload.py: repair ladders, capacity cooldowns, 502/503 mapping, title-page program/department detection against a CATALOG fixture, upload-status/cancel/extract-metadata/jobs-list endpoints, legacy-schema fallbacks.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_prompt_contracts.py` (341 lines) - 79 tests. REPOSITORY-FACT + behaviour. SHARED_RULES (:41-57) is a 12-entry dict of literal prompt substrings cross-multiplied against prompts.ALL_GENERATION_PROMPTS. Also pins prompt-injection fencing, the NO_EVIDENCE_SENTINEL lifecycle, repair-prompt composition, and QUESTION_TYPE_TASKS isolation.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_hardening_fixes.py` (721 lines) - 61 tests, one class per defect id from SYSTEM_IMPROVEMENTS_AND_BUGS_2026-08-03.md. Contains two structural AST/SQL guards: no select('*') anywhere in production modules (:634), and pinned SELECT field lists must equal the columns the committed SQL declares (:701).
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_chat_endpoint_flows.py` (1,406 lines) - 61 tests but the largest file. End-to-end drives of chat._chat_impl with monkeypatched _retrieve_evidence/_invoke_generation: fast paths, repair ladder, guest/session follow-ups, edit-truncation, persistence disclosure, live `kind` stamping, question-type plumbing, cross-lingual translation.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_operations_security.py` (945 lines) - 58 tests. Operations migration text contract, ClamAV outcomes, webhook signing, retention, privacy log redaction (including exc_text), encrypted storage backup round-trip and path-escape guards, worker lifecycle, maintenance-route fail-closed, and PowerShell/nginx file assertions.
- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_durable_ingestion.py` (1,051 lines) - 58 tests. process_ingestion_job happy path and every refusal, retry classification, lease contract, batch upload envelope rules, and TestSqlQueueContracts (:1019) asserting the queue functions/grants/status lists in both supabase_setup.sql and 20260723.

- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_chat_notices.py` (379 lines) - 43 tests. REPOSITORY-FACT. Classifier coverage of every NOTICE_MARKERS entry, the p_kind persistence payload, SQL-side history filtering, and a text-match backfill audit against two migration files. MARKERS_WITHOUT_HISTORICAL_ROWS (:48-67) is a hand-curated exemption set.

- `rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_evaluation_harness.py` (670 lines) - 41 tests. Model-name defaults, gemini_timeout_seconds 60.0, embedding_dimensions 768, exact generation_contract and rag_contract key sets, schema_version == 4, gateway host extraction, and no credential in the serialized manifest.
