Omissions the checker caught:

- `tmp/deck/out/notes.json` has no producer anywhere in the repository. RUN_OF_SHOW.md:10 tells the presenters that every slide's spoken script "is in its PowerPoint speaker notes, and also in `tmp/deck/out/notes.json`". A repo-wide grep for that string matches exactly one file — RUN_OF_SHOW.md itself. build.py writes only `<deck>.build.json` (build.py:692-701); export_slides.ps1 exports PNGs and prints transitions; capture.mjs writes captures and archive_state.json. The map filed this under the "tmp/ does not exist, regenerate first" gotcha, which implies it is regenerable. It is not: no command produces it. (docs/defense/RUN_OF_SHOW.md:10)
- `export_objective2_summary.main()` carries a provenance gate the map's errorHandling omits entirely: it returns 1 with `no checkpoint for run id 5e8fb7f21db6` unless `evaluation/results/checkpoints/5e8fb7f21db6.provenance.json` exists. The comment states the reason — "A wrong RUN_ID would put a plausible-but-false provenance string on screen, so refuse rather than export one that has no checkpoint behind it." This is the only thing binding the hard-coded RUN_ID/RUN_DATE constants to a real run, since `build()` itself reads the pinned SOURCE file and never validates the id against it. (rag-thesis-backend/scripts/export_objective2_summary.py:126-135)
- Two committed evidence files dated the same day disagree on PI-04's status, with no cross-reference in either direction. `docs/evidence/PI_VERIFICATION_2026-07-25.md:15` records `🚧 PI-04 | IMPLEMENTED-UNVERIFIED | The normalized catalog is not yet applied and rolled back in an authorized disposable Supabase project…`. `docs/evidence/pi-04/VERIFICATION_2026-07-25.md:5` records `**VERIFIED.** The normalized CCSICT academic catalog passed reversible migration … against the explicitly authorized disposable Supabase project.` The map asserts PI-04 VERIFIED without noting that the top-level audit still says otherwise — the same supersession-banner gap it correctly flags for the 50-paper claim, in the same file.
- docs/defense/FINAL_DEFENSE_PLAN.md is the one document in the repository that enumerates the release-fingerprint input set correctly. Hard rule 1 lists routers/chat.py, services/retriever.py, services/prompts.py, services/question_types.py, config.py, requirements.txt, requirements.lock, both Dockerfiles, rag-thesis-frontend/package-lock.json and docker-compose.operations.yml — an exact match for release_fingerprint.build_manifest()'s `inputs`, and it omits chunker.py and index_provenance.py precisely where CLAUDE.md:117 wrongly includes them. This matters for the map's open question "should CLAUDE.md be corrected": the correct list already exists in-repo, so the fix is a copy rather than a judgement. (docs/defense/FINAL_DEFENSE_PLAN.md:28-33 vs rag-thesis-backend/scripts/release_fingerprint.py:51-72 vs CLAUDE.md:117)
- verify.py's central honesty rule is narrower than the invariant the map states. "This is the deck's central honesty rule and it is enforced per-slide over all shape text, including table cells" is accurate as far as it goes, but `all_text` is assembled only from `slide.shapes` (including tables via `_runs`); `slide.notes_slide` is read solely to check that notes are non-empty. Since the Objective 2 numbers are typed into content.NOTES[6] rather than formatted from evidence.py, the one place `3.222e-05` is guaranteed to appear on every build is the place the rule does not inspect. (docs/deck/verify.py:59-88)
- `fonts.py`'s network dependency is first-run only, not per-build: `ensure_fonts()` computes `missing = [w for w in weights if not (FONT_DIR / f'{key}-{w}.ttf').exists()]` and skips the fetch when the TTFs are already cached in `tmp/deck/fonts/`. The map's "an offline machine cannot build or even measure the deck" is true of this working tree only because `tmp/` is absent. (docs/deck/fonts.py:18,36-49)

## 5. `ci-repo` - CI, quality gates and repository hygiene

A single GitHub Actions workflow (`.github/workflows/quality.yml`) is the ISO/IEC 25010 evidence instrument for the thesis's Objective 4: six jobs that install hash-verified dependencies, run both test suites with hard coverage floors, lint both codebases to zero messages, build and vulnerability-scan both container images, scan full git history for secrets, and (conditionally) push both coverage reports into SonarQube. Around it sits a hygiene layer whose job is to make a local run reproduce CI byte for byte: `.gitattributes` forces LF into every blob, `.gitignore`/`.dockerignore` keep scratch and PI-08 controlled material out of the tree and the build context, `requirements.lock` + exact npm pins + digest-pinned base images make the dependency set reproducible, and a family of "repository-fact" pytest files fail when the README, the lock, the Dockerfile COPY list or the SQL schema drift from the code. Nothing here is a linter-for-its-own-sake: each threshold is a number that appears in the paper's Table 4 or in a dated evidence bundle.

### Key files

- `C:/Users/Kazuha/Desktop/Thesis-V1/.github/workflows/quality.yml` (342 lines / 14.6 kB) - The only workflow. Six jobs (backend, frontend-audit, frontend, secrets, containers[matrix x2], sonarqube). Every action pinned by commit SHA with a trailing version comment. Workflow-level `permissions: contents: read`; concurrency group cancels superseded PR runs only.
- `C:/Users/Kazuha/Desktop/Thesis-V1/.github/dependabot.yml` (56 lines) - Five ecosystems, all weekly, all `open-pull-requests-limit: 5`, all grouped under a single `patterns: ["*"]` group so each ecosystem opens one PR: pip@/rag-thesis-backend, npm@/rag-thesis-frontend (eslint >=10 ignored), github-actions@/, docker@/rag-thesis-backend, docker@/rag-thesis-frontend.

Both coverage report paths wired. `sonar.qualitygate.wait=true`, `timeout=300`.

### Control flow

A push or PR against `main` fans out into six independent jobs; only `sonarqube` has a dependency edge (`needs: [backend, frontend]`, quality.yml:289), so the other five race. On a PR the concurrency group cancels the previous run; on a push to main it does not, deliberately, because push runs are the dated evidence.

`backend` (quality.yml:31) is the only job that installs Python. `actions/setup-python` is pinned to `3.14.7` and its pip cache is keyed on `requirements.lock`, not requirements.txt -- so a txt-only edit gets a cache hit and installs the stale set, which is precisely the drift `tests/test_dependency_lock.py:84` fails on. Install is `pip install --require-hashes -r requirements.lock`: pip verifies every distribution's SHA-256 and refuses the whole install if a single requirement lacks a hash, which is why `TestEveryLockedPackageIsHashed` (test_dependency_lock.py:108) exists as a cheaper local catch. `pip check` then validates the resolved graph, and `pip-audit --no-deps -r requirements.lock` audits the *lock* rather than the intent file so transitive advisories are in scope -- `--no-deps` is safe only because the lock is already the complete closure. The pytest step carries `--cov-fail-under=85` over exactly seven measurement targets (routers, services, dependencies, workers, main, config, models); `scripts/`, `evaluation/`, `migrations/` and `tests/` are outside the denominator, which `evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md:248` states explicitly. The pylint step has no `--fail-under` and no `--exit-zero`, so it relies on pylint's exit bitmask (1 fatal / 2 error / 4 warning / 8 refactor / 16 convention): *any* emitted message returns non-zero and reds the job. That is the "10.00/10 or bust" gate the evidence tables record.

`frontend-audit` (:83) exists only because `npm audit` conflates real findings with registry failure -- both exit 1. On 2026-09-04 a slow (not down) advisory endpoint answered a 341-package POST after 294 s and took ESLint, unit tests, build, bundle budget and Playwright down with it, and a failed job also discards the caches it would have saved. The current shape: `--fetch-timeout=300000 --fetch-retries=0` (npm's own retry is off because retrying a slow request only multiplies the wait), three attempts, backoff `15 30 0`, and a grep over the captured report for `audit endpoint returned an error|network timeout|ECONNRESET|ETIMEDOUT|EAI_AGAIN|ENOTFOUND` to decide "retry" versus "this is a real advisory, fail now". Three network failures still fail the job -- a gate that could not run is never reported as green. `timeout-minutes: 20` bounds 3 x 5 min + 45 s of backoff.

`frontend` (:140) restores `node_modules` whole from `actions/cache` keyed on `runner.os` + `node24.18.0` + `hashFiles(package-lock.json)`, and runs `npm ci` only on a miss -- `npm ci` was 303 s of a 622 s job. Then the gates run in fixed order: `eslint .` (errors only; the three `warn`-level rules cannot fail it), `test:coverage` (Node's own runner, no vitest/jest anywhere, with `--test-coverage-lines=85 --test-coverage-functions=85 --test-coverage-branches=80` and `--test-coverage-exclude="**/*.test.js"`), a production `vite build` into `dist/`, and `bundle:budget` which reads `dist/index.html`. The budget script (check-bundle-budget.mjs:62-73) derives the eager set from the HTML itself -- module `<script src>`, `rel=modulepreload` and `rel=stylesheet` hrefs -- rather than guessing, gzips each at level 9, and fails if the total exceeds 330 kB, if `useSceneRuntime` exceeds 250 kB, if `OverviewCharts` exceeds 115 kB, if any other emitted chunk exceeds 150 kB, or -- the check that actually matters -- if either named chunk appears in the eager set at all, which is how a `lazy(() => import(...))` silently converted to a static import gets caught. An empty eager parse is also a failure (:83-88), so a changed Vite output format cannot report 0 kB and pass. Finally `npm run test:e2e` shells to `e2e/run-playwright.mjs`, which spawns `e2e/vite-server.mjs` (a programmatic `vite build --mode e2e` into `dist-e2e`, never `dist`, followed by `preview` on 127.0.0.1:4173 with strictPort), polls the port for up to 120 s, runs the Playwright CLI with `PLAYWRIGHT_REUSE_SERVER=1` so `playwright.config.js:28` reuses that server instead of racing a second one on the same strict port, and shuts it down by writing `close\n` to stdin with a 5 s grace before `kill()`.

`containers` (:230) is a 2-way matrix. Each leg does a plain `docker build` from the service directory and then Trivy with `exit-code: '1'`, `ignore-unfixed: true`, `severity: CRITICAL,HIGH` -- fix-available-only, which is exactly why the 21-day-stale Chainguard digest shipping openssl 3.6.3-r3 (CVE-2026-14456) reddened eight consecutive runs even though nothing in the repo serves QUIC. Between build and scan, the backend leg alone runs an import smoke test (`python -c "import main, workers.ingestion_worker"` with placeholder credentials that `config.validate_production_services` accepts under `APP_ENVIRONMENT=test`); it exists because commit da9e931 built green, scanned green and died on `docker run` with ModuleNotFoundError after `warning_filters.py` was added without a Dockerfile COPY entry. Both legs emit an SPDX SBOM through `anchore/sbom-action`, which uploads the artifact itself so there is no separate upload step to keep in sync.

`sonarqube` (:283) gates itself twice. First, an inline shell step: no `SONAR_TOKEN` secret means `enabled=false` and every subsequent step is skipped with a green job. Second, the host. `SONAR_HOST_URL` arrives from a repository *variable* -- a lower bar than the secret it steers, and unlike editing the workflow, changing a variable leaves no git trace -- so the step allowlists the literal string `https://sonarcloud.io` and hard-errors on anything else, then re-exports the vetted value through `steps.sonar.outputs.host` so the scan step never reads `vars` directly. Both coverage artifacts are downloaded into the paths `sonar-project.properties` names; without the frontend lcov the React sources analyse as 0% and pulled the whole-repository figure to 36.3%. `sonar.qualitygate.wait=true` with a 300 s timeout means the job blocks on, and fails with, the server-side quality gate.

The hygiene layer runs outside CI. `.gitattributes`'s `* text=auto eol=lf` normalizes on commit, and the index is verifiably clean: `git ls-files --eol | grep -c 'i/crlf\|i/mixed'` returns 0 across the whole repository. The mechanism that makes CRLF dangerous locally is pylint's raw `mixed-line-endings` (C0327) checker, which `.pylintrc` does not disable; because the pylint step fails on any message, a single mixed-ending file in `routers/ services/ dependencies/ workers/ main.py config.py models.py` turns the local gate red while CI -- which checks out fresh on Linux -- stays green and gives no hint why. `expected-line-ending-format` is left unset, so uniformly-CRLF files do not trigger C0328; only *mixed* files do, which matches the recorded retriever.py incident (476 CRLF lines mixed into 255 LF). This machine has `core.autocrlf=true` set locally, which the `eol=lf` attribute overrides on checkout -- but nine tracked files are currently CRLF in the working tree anyway (written back by an editor after checkout) and `git status` reports them clean because normalization makes the resulting blob identical.

### Public surface

- WORKFLOW TRIGGERS (.github/workflows/quality.yml:9-14): `push: branches: [main]`, `pull_request: branches: [main]`, `workflow_dispatch`. No schedule, no tags, no path filters -- every job runs on every event.
- CONCURRENCY (:26-28): `group: ${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}`, `cancel-in-progress: ${{ github.event_name == 'pull_request' }}`. Pushes to main are never cancelled because those runs are the dated evidence artifacts.
- PERMISSIONS (:21-22): workflow default `contents: read`. Only the `secrets` job overrides (:214-216) adding `pull-requests: read`, because gitleaks-action reads a PR's commits through the API.
- JOB 1 `backend` (:31-81) -- runs-on ubuntu-latest, `defaults.run.working-directory: rag-thesis-backend`. Steps: checkout@11d5960 -> setup-python@a26af69 with `python-version: '3.14.7'`, `cache: pip`, `cache-dependency-path: rag-thesis-backend/requirements.lock` -> `pip install --require-hashes -r requirements.lock` -> `pip check` -> `pip install pip-audit && pip-audit --no-deps -r requirements.lock` -> pytest with `--cov=routers --cov=services --cov=dependencies --cov=workers --cov=main --cov=config --cov=models --cov-report=xml --cov-report=term --cov-fail-under=85` -> `pylint --rcfile=.pylintrc routers services dependencies workers main.py config.py models.py` -> upload-artifact `backend-coverage` = rag-thesis-backend/coverage.xml.
- JOB 2 `frontend-audit` (:83-138) -- isolated on purpose; `timeout-minutes: 20`. checkout -> setup-node 24.18.0 (no cache, no install) -> a hand-rolled retry loop: 3 attempts of `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high --fetch-timeout=300000 --fetch-retries=0`, backoff `15 30 0` seconds. A non-network failure short-circuits to `::error::High or critical advisories in production dependencies.` and exit 1; three network failures end in `::error::npm audit could not reach the advisory endpoint after 3 attempts.` and exit 1.
- JOB 3 `frontend` (:140-205) -- checkout -> setup-node 24.18.0 with `cache: npm` keyed on package-lock.json -> actions/cache@0057852 restoring `rag-thesis-frontend/node_modules` under key `node-modules-${{ runner.os }}-node24.18.0-${{ hashFiles('rag-thesis-frontend/package-lock.json') }}` -> `npm ci --prefer-offline --no-audit --no-fund` ONLY on cache miss -> `npm run lint` -> `npm run test:coverage` -> upload-artifact `frontend-coverage` = coverage/lcov.info -> `npm run build` -> `npm run bundle:budget` -> cache `~/.cache/ms-playwright` keyed on the same lockfile hash -> `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium` -> `npm run test:e2e`.
- JOB 4 `secrets` (:207-228) -- `actions/checkout` with `fetch-depth: 0`, then `gitleaks/gitleaks-action@ff98106` with `GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}`. Full-history scan. No .gitleaks.toml and no GITLEAKS_LICENSE in the repo.
- JOB 5 `containers` (:230-281) -- `strategy.matrix.include` of {backend, rag-thesis-backend} and {frontend, rag-thesis-frontend}. `docker build -t isu-thesis-<name>:ci <context>` -> a backend-only smoke step `docker run --rm -e GEMINI_API_KEY=test-key -e SUPABASE_URL=https://test-project.supabase.co -e SUPABASE_KEY=test-key -e APP_ENVIRONMENT=test isu-thesis-backend:ci python -c "import main, workers.ingestion_worker; print('backend image imports', main.app.version)"` -> `aquasecurity/trivy-action@ed142fd (v0.36.0)` with `format: table`, `exit-code: '1'`, `ignore-unfixed: true`, `severity: CRITICAL,HIGH` -> `anchore/sbom-action@e22c389` emitting `sbom-<name>.spdx.json`.
- JOB 6 `sonarqube` (:283-342) -- `needs: [backend, frontend]` only. checkout `fetch-depth: 0` for blame -> a gate step that reads `secrets.SONAR_TOKEN` and `vars.SONAR_HOST_URL || 'https://sonarcloud.io'`; empty token sets `enabled=false` and exits 0 (graceful skip); a host that is not literally `https://sonarcloud.io` hard-fails with `::error::Refusing to send SONAR_TOKEN to unapproved host` -> download both coverage artifacts into `rag-thesis-backend` and `rag-thesis-frontend/coverage` -> `SonarSource/sonarqube-scan-action@2f77a1e` with `SONAR_HOST_URL: ${{ steps.sonar.outputs.host }}` (the allowlisted value, never the raw repository variable).
- npm scripts that are CI gates (rag-thesis-frontend/package.json:11-22): `lint` = `eslint .`; `test:coverage` = node --test with `--experimental-test-coverage --test-coverage-exclude="**/*.test.js" --test-coverage-lines=85 --test-coverage-functions=85 --test-coverage-branches=80`, lcov to coverage/lcov.info and spec to stdout; `build` = `vite build`; `bundle:budget` = `node scripts/check-bundle-budget.mjs`; `test:e2e` = `node e2e/run-playwright.mjs`.
- npm scripts that are NOT run by CI: `test` (bare `node --test`), `dev`, `preview`, `test:e2e:install`, and `security:headers` (`node scripts/validate-security-headers.mjs <url>`, which needs a live deployment and checks CSP/X-Frame-Options/X-Content-Type-Options/Referrer-Policy/Permissions-Policy and HSTS on https).
- Sonar keys set: `sonar.projectKey=isu-thesis-ai-library`, `sonar.projectName`, `sonar.projectVersion=2.1.0`, `sonar.sources=rag-thesis-backend,rag-thesis-frontend/src`, `sonar.tests=rag-thesis-backend/tests`, `sonar.python.version=3.14`, `sonar.python.coverage.reportPaths=rag-thesis-backend/coverage.xml`, `sonar.javascript.lcov.reportPaths=rag-thesis-frontend/coverage/lcov.info`, `sonar.sourceEncoding=UTF-8`, `sonar.qualitygate.wait=true`, `sonar.qualitygate.timeout=300`. `sonar.organization` is present but COMMENTED OUT (:26).
- Sonar exclusions (sonar-project.properties:39-51), eleven globs: `rag-thesis-backend/.venv*/**`, `rag-thesis-backend/tests/**`, `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/**`, `**/__pycache__/**`, `rag-thesis-frontend/node_modules/**`, `rag-thesis-frontend/dist/**`, `rag-thesis-backend/tmp-*/**`, `rag-thesis-backend/.pytest-*/**`, `rag-thesis-backend/.pytest_cache/**`, `rag-thesis-backend/.pylint-*/**`, `rag-thesis-frontend/src/**/*.test.js`.
- Repository-fact pytest gates and what each pins: `tests/test_readme_accuracy.py` (32 tests) -> README.md + frontend README + jmeter README + evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md; `tests/test_dependency_lock.py` (9 tests) -> requirements.txt <-> requirements.lock; `tests/test_dockerfile_contents.py` (3 tests) -> Dockerfile COPY list vs entrypoint imports; `tests/test_schema_consistency.py` -> supabase_setup.sql vs migrations/; `tests/test_prompt_contracts.py`; `tests/test_chat_notices.py` + `tests/test_auth_authorization.py` -> refusal/notice wording.
- Backend test collection, measured now: `2143 tests collected in 5.36s` (CLAUDE.md's ~2,135 is the count before the 8 uncommitted tests in tests/test_objective2_summary_export.py). 55 test modules under rag-thesis-backend/tests/.
- Frontend test census: 22 colocated `*.test.js` files under src/. E2E: 26 Playwright tests = 13 literal `test(` in e2e/critical-flows.spec.js + 12 generated by `for (const surface of SURFACES)` at e2e/accessibility.spec.js:484 + 1 four-viewport matrix in e2e/visual-quality.spec.js:50.

### Invariants

- Backend statement coverage must be >= 85% over routers, services, dependencies, workers, main, config, models (`--cov-fail-under=85`, quality.yml:72). Current measured value in evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md:20 is 92.07% (5,122 statements, 406 missed).
- Pylint must emit ZERO messages over `routers services dependencies workers main.py config.py models.py`. There is no `--fail-under`, so the gate is pylint's exit bitmask, not the score. Recorded result: 10.00/10.
- Frontend coverage floors, all three simultaneously: lines >= 85, functions >= 85, branches >= 80 (package.json:16). Recorded: 96.36 / 96.81 / 87.09.
- Eager gzipped payload <= 330 kB (check-bundle-budget.mjs:37). Named lazy caps: useSceneRuntime <= 250 kB, OverviewCharts <= 115 kB (:40-43). Any other single chunk <= 150 kB (:46).
- `useSceneRuntime` and `OverviewCharts` must never appear in the eager set (:49, enforced at :118-125). This is the check that catches a lazy import converted to static; the size checks alone would still pass.
- The E2E build must go to `dist-e2e`, never `dist`, because `dist` is what the bundle budget measures (vite-server.mjs:12).
- Python is exactly 3.14.7: asserted in rag-thesis-backend/Dockerfile:27, pinned in quality.yml:47, stated in README.md:74, and printed in the paper's Tables 1-4.
- Every distribution in requirements.lock carries at least one `--hash=sha256:<64 hex>`; `pip install --require-hashes` fails the entire install otherwise. Currently 98 packages, 2,353 hash lines, zero unhashed.
- requirements.txt and requirements.lock must agree on every direct pin after PEP-503 name normalization (`tessdata.eng` == `tessdata-eng`), and the lock must pin each package exactly once (test_dependency_lock.py:84, :97).
- The lock must be resolved for Linux -- its header must contain the string 'linux' (test_dependency_lock.py:132) -- because tesserocr ships manylinux-only wheels and a Windows resolve would omit them.
- cryptography major version >= 50 in BOTH files, in lockstep (test_dependency_lock.py:146).
- Every first-party top-level import of `main.py` and `workers/ingestion_worker.py` must be named by a build-stage `COPY` (test_dockerfile_contents.py:58), with `warning_filters.py` asserted by name (:70).
- Every text blob is stored LF (`* text=auto eol=lf`). Verified: 0 of the tracked files have a CRLF or mixed index blob.
- Sonar's `sonar.tests` and `sonar.sources` must stay disjoint, and `sonar.test.inclusions` must NOT be added -- it filters the whole test file set, and `**/*.test.js` once dropped Python files analysed from 102 to 59 (sonar-project.properties:31-36).
- The Sonar venv exclusion glob must stay wildcarded as `.venv*` -- the specific glob `.venv/**` once let a `.venv3146` directory be analysed as project code (sonar-project.properties:53-58).
- `SONAR_TOKEN` may only be sent to `https://sonarcloud.io` (quality.yml:312-316). Any other value from the `SONAR_HOST_URL` repository variable hard-fails the job.
- Only the `secrets` job may exceed `contents: read`, and only by adding `pull-requests: read` (quality.yml:214-216).
- docker-compose.operations.yml forces `APP_ENVIRONMENT=production` on both the api and worker services, which makes `config.validate_production_services` refuse to start without Redis, privileged MFA, ClamAV and a non-zero guest token budget. Both also gate on `clamav: {condition: service_healthy}`.
- All five Dependabot ecosystems use a single `patterns: ["*"]` group, so each opens exactly one grouped PR per week; eslint `>=10` is held back because eslint-plugin-react caps its peer range at eslint ^9.7 and an ERESOLVE takes the whole group PR down.
- Every GitHub Action is pinned by 40-char commit SHA with a trailing `# v<major>` comment; Dependabot's `github-actions` ecosystem at `/` keeps them moving.
- Both Dockerfiles pin their base images by digest, not tag. Chainguard's free tier retains only the newest build, so a stale backend digest eventually fails to pull with 'manifest unknown' -- a loud failure, which is the design intent.

### Data model

- No database. The artifacts CI produces and consumes are: `backend-coverage` (workflow artifact containing rag-thesis-backend/coverage.xml, Cobertura XML), `frontend-coverage` (coverage/lcov.info), `sbom-backend.spdx.json` and `sbom-frontend.spdx.json` (SPDX-JSON, uploaded by anchore/sbom-action itself).
- GitHub Actions cache entries: `node-modules-${{ runner.os }}-node24.18.0-${{ hashFiles('rag-thesis-frontend/package-lock.json') }}` for rag-thesis-frontend/node_modules, `playwright-${{ runner.os }}-${{ hashFiles(...package-lock.json) }}` for ~/.cache/ms-playwright, plus setup-python's pip cache keyed on rag-thesis-backend/requirements.lock and setup-node's npm cache keyed on package-lock.json.
- Secrets and variables consumed: `secrets.SONAR_TOKEN` (optional; absence skips the scan), `secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN` (gitleaks only), `vars.SONAR_HOST_URL` (optional, allowlisted to https://sonarcloud.io).
- requirements.lock structure: a two-line uv header naming the exact regeneration command, then 98 records of `name==version \` followed by one or more ` --hash=sha256:<64 hex> \` lines and a ` # via <parents>` comment block. 2,353 hash lines total; no line falls outside {comment, blank, --hash, name==version}.
- rag-thesis-frontend/src/data/objective2Summary.json (untracked, 2,595 bytes): `{_generated_by, _do_not_edit, run:{id,date,generated_at,git_commit,prompt_version,formal_result,queries_scored,queries_total,department,framework,judge_model,answer_model,metric,evidence_path}, quoted_stratum, strata:[{key,label,baseline,rag,n,delta,p_value,significant,cohens_d_z,ci_95,quoted}], diagnostics:{<name>:{mean,n}}}`. Five strata: pooled, present, absent_topic, absent_unreleased, absent_by_design.

### Config knobs

- `SONAR_TOKEN` (repository secret) — absent: the sonarqube job's gate step writes `enabled=false`, every later step is skipped, and the job is green. Present: the scan runs and blocks on the server quality gate.
- `SONAR_HOST_URL` (repository *variable*, defaults to https://sonarcloud.io) — any other value hard-fails the job by design; the comment at quality.yml:305-311 explains that a variable is a lower bar than the secret it steers and leaves no git trace.
- `CI` (set by GitHub) — read by playwright.config.js:14-16 to switch workers 1 -> 2, retries 0 -> 2, and enable forbidOnly.
- `PLAYWRIGHT_REUSE_SERVER=1` — set only by e2e/run-playwright.mjs:46; controls whether Playwright reuses the already-running preview server instead of starting its own.
- `--mode e2e` / `import.meta.env.MODE === 'e2e'` — switches the app onto deterministic fixtures, rewrites VITE_API_URL to `/__e2e_api`, blanks the Turnstile site key, and installs the fail-closed 501 guard on both the dev and preview servers (vite.config.js:50-67, :79-88).
- `DECK_THEME` (light|dark) and the new `--motif {orb,logo}` flag on docs/deck/build.py.
- docker-compose.operations.yml environment overrides applied to both Python processes: APP_ENVIRONMENT=production, RATE_LIMIT_STORAGE_URI=redis://redis:6379/0, MALWARE_SCAN_MODE=clamav, CLAMAV_HOST=clamav, OPERATIONS_MONITOR_ENABLED=true. Frontend build args: VITE_API_URL/VITE_SUPABASE_URL/VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY/VITE_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY, sourced from PUBLIC_API_ORIGIN and the VITE_* host env; runtime env API_ORIGIN, SUPABASE_ORIGIN, SUPABASE_WS_ORIGIN and `DOLLAR: $$` for the nginx template.
- Frontend Dockerfile ARGs are baked at build time, so changing any VITE_* value requires a rebuild, not a restart.
- `ALLOW_DISPOSABLE_SUPABASE_TESTS=1` — opts tests/test_supabase_rls_integration.py in; it skips otherwise, which is why iso25010_evidence.md:247 requires the evidence table to state which convention a test count used.

### Error handling

- Backend job: any non-zero exit fails the job. pytest fails on test failure OR on coverage below 85. pylint fails on ANY emitted message (exit bitmask, no --fail-under). pip-audit fails on any advisory in the lock. `--require-hashes` fails the install outright if a single requirement lacks a hash.
- frontend-audit: distinguishes advisory findings (fail immediately, with `::error::High or critical advisories in production dependencies.`) from endpoint failures (retry up to 3, then `::error::npm audit could not reach the advisory endpoint after 3 attempts.` and exit 1). It never passes an unverified tree.
- Bundle budget: collects ALL violations and prints every one before exiting 1, so a single run tells you everything. It also fails on a parse that yields zero eager assets -- a silent 0 kB pass is treated as worse than a failure (check-bundle-budget.mjs:83-88). The failure message tells you to raise the budget in the same commit so the change is reviewable.
- Playwright: 2 retries in CI, trace/screenshot/video retained on failure, HTML report in playwright-report/. e2e/global-setup.js clears test-results/axe-shards/ once per run so a renamed or removed surface cannot leave a stale shard padding the merged accessibility report.
- e2e API guard: an unmocked request in E2E mode returns HTTP 501 `{detail: 'Unmocked E2E API request'}` rather than falling through the SPA history fallback and answering 200 with index.html (which surfaced as a confusing JSON parse error).
- run-playwright.mjs: watches for premature server exit (`The E2E web server stopped before becoming ready`) and times out after 120 s. The `finally` block always attempts a graceful stdin `close`, falling back to `kill()` after 5 s.
- Trivy: `exit-code: '1'` on CRITICAL/HIGH, `ignore-unfixed: true`. Fix-available-only means a stale base-image digest reddens the job until Dependabot moves it; an expired Chainguard digest fails earlier still, at pull, with 'manifest unknown'.
- Container smoke test: `docker run ... python -c "import main, workers.ingestion_worker"` catches a missing Dockerfile COPY entry at CI time, which neither the build nor Trivy can see.
- Sonar: `sonar.qualitygate.wait=true` with `sonar.qualitygate.timeout=300` blocks and fails the job on a server-side quality-gate failure or a 5-minute timeout.
- Repository-fact tests fail with actionable messages rather than assertions: the lock test prints the exact `uv pip compile` command, the Dockerfile test names the missing module and says 'the container would fail at import', the README test lists the broken relative links.

### Tests that pin it

- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_dependency_lock.py — 9 tests, 5 classes. Pins txt<->lock version agreement, single-pin-per-package, universal hashing, sha256 well-formedness, the Linux resolve target, presence of tesserocr + tessdata-eng, and cryptography >= 50 in both files. `test_the_parsers_are_not_vacuous` guards the guards (>=20 direct pins, lock strictly larger, fastapi in both).
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_dockerfile_contents.py — 3 tests (2 parametrized over the two entrypoints + the named warning_filters.py regression). Only covers direct entrypoint imports at COPY granularity.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_readme_accuracy.py — 32 tests, 8 classes: migration-only tables named in the README, upload_jobs in the base schema, the baseline scored only on answer_correctness (asserted at the two `baseline_entry = {` construction sites in run_comparison.py, not at the append), every relative README link resolves, the four JMeter plans exist and are named, jmeter README uses ApacheJMeter.jar not `jmeter -n -t`, /chat runs point at summarize_chat_load, npm version matches packageManager, the admin tab count matches Admin.jsx's `lazy(() => import('./admin/` count, test:coverage and bundle:budget are documented, stale evidence figures are not left in the current table, and both Sonar coverage report paths are configured.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_schema_consistency.py (272 lines) — pins the known base-schema vs migration differences so a `create or replace function` body changed in one file and not the other is caught.
- rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_objective2_summary_export.py (UNTRACKED, 8 tests) — asserts the committed JSON equals `build()` exactly, is LF with a trailing newline, carries the formal run's provenance (5e8fb7f21db6, 2026-09-13, formal_result true, 40/40, ragas==0.4.3), matches the evidence table's pooled figures (0.2169 -> 0.3024, p=3.222e-05), and that the quoted `present` stratum stays n=16, delta +0.0601, p=0.1257, NOT significant with a CI straddling zero. All 8 pass locally.
- rag-thesis-frontend/e2e/critical-flows.spec.js — 13 tests. rag-thesis-frontend/e2e/accessibility.spec.js — 12 tests generated from a `SURFACES` array (landing, authentication, guest-chat, dashboard, archive, novelty, upload, upload-batch, admin-overview, admin-upload-history, admin-system-management, admin-operations) with @axe-core/playwright, sharded to test-results/axe-shards/. rag-thesis-frontend/e2e/visual-quality.spec.js — 1 test iterating four viewports x N routes. Total 26, matching README.md:400 and iso25010_evidence.md:25.
- 22 colocated frontend `*.test.js` under src/ (design/, lib/, pages/{archive,auth,chat,novelty,upload}/, components/three/). Run by Node's built-in runner; `.spec.js` files are not matched by Node's default test glob, so the Playwright specs never leak into `node --test`.
- Backend suite: 2,143 tests collected across 55 modules (2,135 before the uncommitted export test file).

### Gotchas

- `rag-thesis-backend/probe7b.py` is a TRACKED scratch probe script at the backend root, added in commit 576f59f. It `exec()`s `/tmp/probe6.py`, reads `/tmp/targets.txt`, contains dead code (`TARGETS=[...] if False else None`), and is CRLF in the working tree. It escapes every gate: pytest ignores it (testpaths=tests), pylint ignores it (explicit module list), coverage ignores it -- but `sonar.sources=rag-thesis-backend` with no exclusion for it means SonarQube DOES analyse it as project code. None of the .gitignore scratch patterns (`_repro*`, `*_tmp.py`, `tmp_*.py`, `tmp-*`) match the name `probe7b.py`.
- The local `.venv` is Python **3.14.6**, not the 3.14.7 that CI pins, the Dockerfile asserts and README.md:74 mandates. The whole `.venv`-naming and LF-normalization apparatus exists so a local run reproduces CI exactly; a minor-version-off interpreter quietly undermines that.
- `rag-thesis-frontend/src/data/` is UNTRACKED while the tracked, modified `AdminOverview.jsx` now does `import OBJECTIVE2 from '../../data/objective2Summary.json'`. Committing the .jsx without `git add rag-thesis-frontend/src/data/` gives a repo whose `npm run build` fails. ESLint will not catch it: the flat config has no import resolver plugin, so `eslint .` passes on a non-existent module path.
- `tests/test_objective2_summary_export.py:30` has `pytestmark = pytest.mark.skipif(not SOURCE.exists() or not OUT.exists())`. The gate designed to stop the committed JSON drifting from the run SKIPS SILENTLY the moment either file is absent -- i.e. exactly in the scenario where the JSON was never committed, or where someone `git rm --cached`s the source run. The 8 tests turn into 8 skips, not 8 failures.
- `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/` is gitignored (backend/.gitignore:23) yet 13 files under it are force-tracked, including `comparison_20260913_064053.json` -- the source the new export script and its test depend on. This works today only because someone used `git add -f`; the ignore rule means a future contributor regenerating results will not see them in `git status`.
- `test_dockerfile_contents.py` only resolves the *direct* top-level imports of the two entrypoint files. A new root-level module imported transitively (only by, say, `services/foo.py`) passes the test and still fails the container at import. Worse, `is_copied()` (:51-54) treats a package as covered if ANY path under it is copied -- the Dockerfile copies only `scripts/gemini_release_smoke.py`, so an entrypoint importing any other `scripts.*` module would pass the test and ModuleNotFoundError at runtime.
- `sonar-project.properties:11` documents the coverage command WITHOUT `--cov=workers`, while quality.yml:70 and README.md:289 both include it. Following the properties file's own instructions produces a coverage.xml that reports the worker as uncovered.
- `sonar.organization` is commented out (sonar-project.properties:26) but the CI host allowlist permits only `https://sonarcloud.io`. Setting the `SONAR_TOKEN` secret today would run the scan against SonarCloud, which requires `sonar.organization`, so the job would fail on first enable. The two halves of the SonarCloud path have never been exercised together.
- ESLint's three maintainability rules -- `complexity: ['warn', 24]`, `max-depth: ['warn', 5]`, `no-console: ['warn', ...]` -- are warnings, and `npm run lint` is bare `eslint .` with no `--max-warnings 0`. They are advisory, not gates. iso25010_evidence.md:530 records a passing run with 1 warning.
- Nine tracked files are CRLF in the working tree right now while their index blobs are LF, so `git status` shows them clean: AGENT.md, docs/evidence/security/audit-2026-09-05/pip-audit.json, rag-thesis-backend/probe7b.py, rag-thesis-frontend/{eslint.config.js, playwright.config.js, vite.config.js}, and src/{components/ui/Pagination.jsx, pages/archive/ArchiveFiltersBar.jsx, pages/archive/useArchiveCatalog.js}. None are in pylint's target list, so the C0327 trap is not currently sprung -- but the mechanism is live and one CRLF write into services/ or routers/ reproduces the documented 10.00 -> 9.94 failure.
- `core.autocrlf=true` is set in this clone's local git config. It is neutralized by the `eol=lf` attribute, but the two settings are in direct conflict and the README/.gitattributes narrative never mentions that autocrlf is on.
- `docker-compose.operations.yml` pins `redis:8.6.4-alpine3.23` and `clamav/clamav:1.5.3-debian13-slim` by TAG, not digest, and Dependabot's two `docker` ecosystem entries point at `/rag-thesis-backend` and `/rag-thesis-frontend` only. The compose file is at the repository root and is covered by no ecosystem, so those two images float unmanaged and unscanned -- they are also never built or Trivy-scanned by the `containers` job.
- `rag-thesis-backend/.dockerignore` excludes `evaluation/results/` but not `evaluation/` itself (49 MB) and not `evaluation/corpus/private/`, which exists on disk and is the PI-08 controlled corpus. That material is shipped into the Docker build context on every `docker build`. It never reaches the image (the Dockerfile uses an explicit COPY list), but it is sent to the daemon and would be captured by any build-context-capturing tooling.
- `rag-thesis-frontend/Dockerfile:8` is `COPY . .` and the frontend `.dockerignore` omits `dist-e2e/`, `coverage/`, `e2e/`, `.nvmrc` and the Playwright config. Local E2E and coverage output lands in the build layer. Secrets are safe (`.env` and `.env.*` are excluded with a `!.env.example` re-include), but the layer is larger than it needs to be and the cache busts on unrelated local test runs.
- `npm run test:e2e` is the only supported way to run Playwright. Invoking `npx playwright test` directly leaves `PLAYWRIGHT_REUSE_SERVER` unset, so `reuseExistingServer` is false and Playwright's own `webServer` tries to start a second `e2e/vite-server.mjs` on strictPort 4173 -- an immediate EADDRINUSE if one is already up.
- `npm run bundle:budget` reads `dist/index.html` and exits 1 with 'no build found' if `npm run build` has not run. Because the E2E build writes to `dist-e2e`, running the E2E suite first does NOT satisfy it; and a stale `dist/` from an earlier commit would be measured silently.
- The `sonarqube` job depends only on `backend` and `frontend`. A green Sonar result is compatible with a red `secrets`, red `containers` or red `frontend-audit`. Whether that matters depends on branch protection, which is not expressed in this repository.
- The npm-audit retry loop classifies failures by grepping npm's own output for network strings. A genuine advisory whose package name or description happens to contain e.g. 'network timeout' would be misclassified as a transient failure, retried twice, and then reported as an endpoint failure rather than a vulnerability.
- `htmlcov/` is excluded by `rag-thesis-backend/.dockerignore:22` but appears in no `.gitignore` and in no Sonar exclusion, so a local `coverage html` run produces committable, analysable generated HTML.
- The root `.gitignore:39` blanket-ignores `*.sql` with only two negations (`rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql` and `rag-thesis-backend/migrations/*.sql`). Any new `.sql` anywhere else -- a fixture, an evidence dump, a second migrations directory -- is silently untracked.
- `paper/figures/figure_ai_pipeline.png` (329 kB, untracked) is referenced by nothing: the two new `paper/Figure_*.build.py` scripts emit `.svg`, and `paper/build_corrections.py` does not mention it. It is an orphan render sitting next to two tracked figure PNGs.
- `rag-thesis-backend/scripts/export_objective2_summary.py` (new, untracked) is outside both the coverage denominator and the pylint target list, but inside `sonar.sources`. It therefore ships with Sonar's rules applied and neither of the two gates that actually block a merge.
- `docs/deck/build.py`'s new `--motif logo` path rebinds the module-level `RENDERS` inside `main()` via `global`. That is consistent with `RENDERS = theme.RENDER_DIR` at :39 (both are directory Paths), but it only takes effect through `main()` -- importing and calling `build(args)` directly, as another script might, leaves RENDERS pointing at the orb set while `ctx.renders` holds logo hashes, which would make the provenance sidecar disagree with the embedded art. The docstring at :113-119 flags this exact hazard for the sidecar.
- `scripts/verify-pi03-*.ps1` and `scripts/verify-pi04-live.ps1` at the repository root, `paper/*.py`, and `docs/deck/*.py` are outside `sonar.sources`, outside the pylint target list, outside the coverage scope and outside pytest's `testpaths`. Roughly 100 kB of Python and PowerShell in the artifact is ungated.
- No `.trivyignore`, no `.gitleaks.toml`, no `.editorconfig`, no `CODEOWNERS`, no pre-commit hooks. The LF invariant, the commit-message convention and the 'regenerate the lock in the same commit' rule are enforced only by review and by the repository-fact tests -- nothing runs before a commit lands.

### Open questions

- Is `rag-thesis-backend/probe7b.py` intended to be in the artifact? Everything about it (exec of /tmp/probe6.py, `if False else None`, hard-coded absolute temp paths) says no, and the repository's own .gitignore comments describe removing exactly this shape twice before. It is currently the one file Sonar analyses that nobody owns.
- Was the local `.venv` meant to stay on 3.14.6 after CI and the Dockerfile moved to 3.14.7 on 2026-08-25? README.md:74 says to use 3.14.7.
- Is the SonarCloud path ever expected to run? `sonar.organization` is commented out while the CI host allowlist permits only sonarcloud.io, so enabling SONAR_TOKEN today would fail rather than scan.
- Should `docker-compose.operations.yml`'s redis and clamav images be digest-pinned and covered by Dependabot? They are the only container images in the artifact that are neither digest-pinned nor Trivy-scanned.
- The skipif in tests/test_objective2_summary_export.py:30 converts the missing-file case from a failure into a skip. Was that intended, given the test exists precisely to stop the file going missing or stale?
- DEFENSE_WALKTHROUGH.md's pending edit flags an unexplained fourth-decimal disagreement between the export and iso25010_evidence.md on the two n<5 strata, and says it is 'worth chasing after the defense'. That is an open numeric discrepancy in the paper's evidence chain, currently papered over by rendering three decimals.
- Is `paper/figures/figure_ai_pipeline.png` a leftover, or is a paper edit that references it still pending?
- Whether branch protection makes all six jobs required checks is not knowable from the repository; the `sonarqube` job's narrow `needs` only matters if it is.

**Depends on:** GitHub Actions hosted ubuntu-latest runners with Docker available, PyPI (hash-verified), and pypi.org for the pip-audit install itself, which is NOT hash-pinned, npm registry + npm's bulk advisory endpoint (the single most failure-prone external dependency in the pipeline), cgr.dev/chainguard/python (free tier retains only the newest build), docker.io for node:24.18.0-alpine3.24 and nginx:1.31.3-alpine3.24, sonarcloud.io, only when SONAR_TOKEN is set, Playwright's CDN for the Chromium download on a cache miss, Trivy's vulnerability database and anchore/syft, rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/comparison_20260913_064053.json — force-tracked inside a gitignored directory; the new export script and its test both read it

**Used by:** evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md — the dated revalidation tables quote this workflow's commands and their exit codes verbatim; tests/test_readme_accuracy.py::TestEvidenceFiguresAreNotStale keeps them from drifting, README.md:392-404 — the CI job table, pinned by tests/test_readme_accuracy.py, paper/build_corrections.py — Tables 1-4 are regenerated from requirements.txt version pins and the Dockerfile's interpreter assertion, scripts/release_fingerprint.py — hashes source inputs (services/prompts.py, chunker, index_provenance) and records runtime + lock provenance; the same pinning discipline underpins it, scripts/verify-pi03-dependencies.ps1, verify-pi03-container-security.ps1, verify-pi03-multiarch.ps1, verify-pi03-gemini.ps1, verify-pi04-live.ps1 — the local PowerShell counterparts of the CI dependency and container checks, docs/evidence/security/audit-2026-09-05/ — retained pip-audit output from a dated run

**Adversarial fact-check:** verdict `mostly-accurate`, 11 correction(s), 11 omission(s). Corrections are already folded into the text above. They are kept because each records a wrong belief a competent reader reached from the same files - the same trap is available to you.

- **Wrong:** tests/test_readme_accuracy.py — "32 tests over 8 classes"
  **Actual:** 32 collected tests is CORRECT (verified: `pytest tests/test_readme_accuracy.py --collect-only -q` → 32). The class count is 9, not 8: TestTheReadmeExistsAndIsParsed, TestFreshProjectSetupIsComplete, TestObjectiveTwoMetricsAreDescribedCorrectly, TestReferencedFilesExist, TestFrontendCommandsResolve, TestFrontendReadmeMatchesTheFrontend, TestJmeterReadmeIsRunnable, TestEvidenceFiguresAreNotStale, TestSonarQubeVersionIsHonest. (`rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_readme_accuracy.py:49,60,94,119,147,158,198,228,250`)
- **Wrong:** package.json — "Every one of 27 dependencies and 12 devDependencies is an exact version"
  **Actual:** There are 26 dependencies, not 27 (12 devDependencies is correct). The exact-pinning claim itself holds: no `^` or `~` anywhere in either block. (`rag-thesis-frontend/package.json:23-50`)
- **Wrong:** "Nine tracked files are CRLF in the working tree right now" followed by a 9-item list
  **Actual:** Eleven. `git ls-files --eol | grep 'w/crlf'` returns 11 rows. The map's list omits `rag-thesis-frontend/.gitignore` and `scripts/verify-pi03-dependencies.ps1`. The map's conclusion still holds — none of the 11 sit in pylint's target list (routers/ services/ dependencies/ workers/ main.py config.py models.py), so the C0327 trap is armed but not sprung. The index is clean: `git ls-files --eol | grep -c 'i/crlf\|i/mixed'` = 0. (`git ls-files --eol; .gitattributes:12`)
- **Wrong:** Root .gitignore "Notable shapes: … `.venv*/`"
  **Actual:** The pattern is path-scoped: `rag-thesis-backend/.venv*/`. A venv created at the repository root or under rag-thesis-frontend/ is NOT ignored — verified: `git check-ignore -v .venv` exits 1 (no match). The backend's own .gitignore:9 has the unscoped `.venv*/`, which is what actually covers the documented `.venv`. (`.gitignore:70 vs rag-thesis-backend/.gitignore:9`)
- **Wrong:** "Roughly 100 kB of Python and PowerShell in the artifact is ungated"
  **Actual:** ~272 kB. Measured: scripts/*.ps1 = 24,598 B; paper/*.py = 121,389 B; docs/deck/*.py = 125,919 B. The underlying point (all of it outside sonar.sources, the pylint module list, the coverage scope and pytest's testpaths) is correct. (`scripts/, paper/, docs/deck/`)
- **Wrong:** "Sonar exclusions (sonar-project.properties:39-51), eleven globs" and "the venv exclusion glob … (sonar-project.properties:53-58)"
  **Actual:** Eleven globs is correct, but they span :39-50 (line 50 is the last glob, `rag-thesis-frontend/src/**/*.test.js`; :51 is blank). The venv-glob rationale comment is at :52-57, not :53-58. (`sonar-project.properties:39-50,52-57`)
- **Wrong:** "`scripts/`, `evaluation/`, `migrations/` and `tests/` are outside the denominator, which `evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md:248` states explicitly"
  **Actual:** Line 248 names only `evaluation/`: "`evaluation/` sits outside both the coverage scope and the CI lint target, which name". It says nothing about scripts/, migrations/ or tests/. Those are outside the denominator (true, by the seven `--cov=` targets at quality.yml:70-71) but the cited line does not state it. (`rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md:247-248; .github/workflows/quality.yml:70-71`)
- **Wrong:** docs/deck/build.py — "The docstring at :113-119 flags this exact hazard for the sidecar"
  **Actual:** Wrong anchor. The docstring at :115-120 (`logo_renders`) explains why missing motif files raise rather than fall back to the orb — nothing about the sidecar. The sidecar hazard is a comment at :148-150, directly above `self.renders = orb.render_all() if args.motif == 'orb' else logo_renders()` (:151). The substance of the gotcha is correct: `global RENDERS` is rebound only inside `main()` (:722-723), while `ctx.renders` is motif-aware at :151. (`docs/deck/build.py:115-120, :148-151, :722-723`)
- **Wrong:** rag-thesis-frontend/.dockerignore — "10 lines: .env/.env.* (with `!.env.example`), node_modules/, dist/, playwright-report/, test-results/, .git/, *.log"
  **Actual:** The enumeration omits `.gitignore` at line 9. 10 lines is correct. The derived gotcha (omits dist-e2e/, coverage/, e2e/, .nvmrc, playwright.config.js while the Dockerfile does `COPY . .` at :8) is confirmed. (`rag-thesis-frontend/.dockerignore:9; rag-thesis-frontend/Dockerfile:8`)
- **Wrong:** eslint.config.js — "Errors: no-unused-vars, no-var, prefer-const, eqeqeq smart, no-return-assign, react/jsx-uses-vars, react/jsx-no-undef, no-alert, no-debugger"
  **Actual:** That is only the explicit `rules` block (:30-52). The config also `extends` js.configs.recommended, reactHooks.configs.flat.recommended and reactRefresh.configs.vite (:15-19), each contributing further error-level rules — notably every react-hooks rule. The map also omits the second config object at :54-60, which turns `react-refresh/only-export-components` off for `**/context/**/*.jsx`, `**/components/ui/Motion.jsx` and `**/pages/auth/AuthFx.jsx`. The gotcha that the three `warn` rules are advisory (bare `eslint .`, no `--max-warnings 0`) is correct. (`rag-thesis-frontend/eslint.config.js:15-19,30-52,54-60; package.json:14`)
- **Wrong:** docker-compose.operations.yml — "Four services (redis, clamav, api, worker) + frontend"
  **Actual:** Five services. Two omissions matter: api and worker each also declare `redis: {condition: service_started}` (:30, :45), and both load `env_file: ./rag-thesis-backend/.env` (:21, :37) — a gitignored file that must exist or `docker compose` refuses to start, so the "forced" environment block is an override layer, not the whole environment. (`docker-compose.operations.yml:19-47`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- pip-audit poisons the hash-verified environment before the gating steps. `pip install pip-audit` (.github/workflows/quality.yml:65) is unpinned and unhashed, and it installs INTO the same environment that :55 just built with `pip install --require-hashes -r requirements.lock`. It runs before pytest (:68-72) and pylint (:74-75). pip-audit's closure (packaging, requests, cyclonedx-python-lib, …) can add or upgrade distributions in that venv, so the comment at :51-53 — "CI installs the same bytes the container does" — is only true up to line 64. The map notes pip-audit is not hash-pinned under dependsOn but never connects it to the two steps that actually gate the merge.
- Only one of six jobs is time-bounded. `timeout-minutes` appears exactly once in the workflow, at :87 (frontend-audit). The `frontend` job — npm ci, lint, coverage, vite build, bundle budget, a Playwright Chromium download and 26 E2E tests — has no timeout, nor do `containers` (docker build + Trivy DB fetch) or `secrets` (full-history gitleaks). All three fall back to GitHub's 6-hour default.
- check-bundle-budget.mjs has a fifth failure mode the map's four-check summary omits: an href that index.html preloads but that is absent from dist/ is pushed as a violation (rag-thesis-frontend/scripts/check-bundle-budget.mjs:96-98). That is the check that catches a stale or half-cleaned dist/, which is precisely the scenario the map's own 'stale dist would be measured silently' gotcha worries about.
- check-bundle-budget.mjs:128 does `readdirSync(join(DIST_DIR, 'assets'))` with no existsSync guard — a build that changes `build.assetsDir` throws ENOENT out of main() instead of reporting a budget violation, so the gate fails with a stack trace rather than an actionable message.
- The eager budget compares the ROUNDED kB value: `if (kb(eagerBytes) > EAGER_BUDGET_KB)` (check-bundle-budget.mjs:112), where `kb` rounds to one decimal. 330.04 kB passes. Not material in practice, but it is not the byte-exact gate the invariant implies.
- rag-thesis-frontend/.gitignore is in the task's file scope and has no keyFiles row at all. 38 lines. It ignores dist/, dist-e2e/, dist-ssr/, playwright-report/, test-results/, blob-report/, playwright/.cache/, playwright/.auth/, *.trace.zip, .env/.env.* with !.env.example — but NOT coverage/, which is ignored from the root file instead (.gitignore:46 `rag-thesis-frontend/coverage/`). It is also itself one of the eleven CRLF working-tree files.
- probe7b.py is worse than 'dead code': it cannot even import. Line 25 `TARGETS=[...] if False else None` evaluates unconditionally to None, and line 26 `exec(open('/tmp/probe6.py').read()...)` is what rebinds it — so on any machine without /tmp/probe6.py the module raises FileNotFoundError at import. It also does `sys.path.insert(0, '.')` and `from tests.test_guards_multilingual import MUST_ALLOW, MUST_REFUSE` (rag-thesis-backend/probe7b.py:2-4), i.e. a tracked artifact file imports the test suite.
- requirements.lock has ZERO blank lines. Exact composition: 2,653 total = 202 comment + 2,353 --hash + 98 name==version. Verified by inverting the grep — no line falls outside those three classes. The map's '{comment, blank, --hash, name==version}' set overstates by including blanks.
- Root .gitignore:39-42 carries THREE negations, not two: `!rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql`, `!rag-thesis-backend/migrations/`, and `!rag-thesis-backend/migrations/*.sql`. The bare-directory negation on line 41 is load-bearing — git will not re-include a file inside a directory that is itself excluded, so dropping it would silently untrack every migration.
- The Sonar-analysed-but-ungated surface is much larger than probe7b.py. `sonar.sources=rag-thesis-backend` with only `evaluation/results/**` excluded means the whole `evaluation/` harness (49 MB on disk, including the Objective 2 runner) and the entire `rag-thesis-backend/scripts/` tree are analysed as project code while sitting outside both the `--cov=` denominator (quality.yml:70-71) and the pylint module list (:75). probe7b.py is the most egregious instance, not the only one.
- Confirmed but unstated as a check: `npx playwright test --list` returns exactly 26 tests in 3 files, and the 12 accessibility surface names match the map's list verbatim (landing, authentication, guest-chat, dashboard, archive, novelty, upload, upload-batch, admin-overview, admin-upload-history, admin-system-management, admin-operations). Note playwright.config.js:11 still says 'Measured on the 24-test suite' — the worker-count rationale comment is two tests stale relative to the suite it justifies.
